# The M0-M7 audit

Ten independent auditors read `doc/Messaging.md`'s milestones clause by clause
against the tree. Every finding was then handed to a separate skeptic whose
instruction was to refuse it -- to check each quote at its cited line, try to
reach the failure, and look for the mitigation the reporter had missed.

**35 confirmed, 32 refuted, 77 agents.** A little under half the raw output did
not survive scrutiny, which is the number to remember about this kind of sweep:
an unverified finding list, from an agent or a person, is a hypothesis list.

This file is here because the evidence is worth more than the summary. Section 18
of `doc/Messaging.md` is the summary -- grouped, deduplicated, and written for
someone deciding what to do next. This is the working record underneath it: for
each finding, the quotes at their line numbers, the failure traced to a caller,
a proposed fix, and the skeptic's verdict including where the reporter's own
anchors were wrong. Several findings were filed against a stale worktree copy and
the verdict says so; the corrections are part of the evidence.

The refuted section is the half most worth keeping. It is what stops the next
session re-litigating a finding that was already checked and found stale, and
several of its entries are better documentation of a subsystem than anything else
in the tree.

Statuses were assigned by hand against the tree as it stands, not by the audit.
`fixed` means the fix is in and a named test fails when it is removed. **30 of the
35 confirmed findings are fixed and 5 are open**, and the counts are computed from
the table below rather than written down beside it.

The five are **1, 5, 12, 14 and 25**, and they are what is left of profile C —
hibernation at scale. Finding 0, the one that made the switch necessary, is fixed. Every finding on profile A's path and profile B's is fixed,
so the shape of what is left is no longer a list but a single decision: whether
this project supports hibernation at scale, which 18.2 says a deployment may
decline. Nothing outside that block is open.

Provenance: the ten audits and their verifiers ran as one orchestrated sweep on
2026-08-10 against the tree at `55966a6`, and this file is rendered from that run's
output. The run itself is gone with the container it ran in, which is why the
evidence is in the repository rather than behind a link.

Duplicates are kept rather than merged: five defects were found independently by
two or three audits that could not see each other, and that agreement is part of
what the evidence shows.

## Confirmed findings

<a id="f0"></a>

### 0. Thread record drops u2.funcidx, so error recovery in a restored thread closes from the stack base

`src/dsnap.c`:1385 &middot; blocks-building-on-it &middot; correctness &middot; audit `m6`

**Status: fixed.** `diluvium_shim_buildframes` reconstructs `u2.funcidx` for every `CIST_YPCALL` frame as `savestack()` of the next frame's function slot, and refuses a `CIST_YPCALL` frame with no callee. No format change, so no snapshot is invalidated. `a_restored_program_can_raise` (`dv_check`) is the test that never existed -- **nothing in the tree had ever resumed a restored thread into an error**, which is the whole reason this was green.

**Measured, three ways, because the symptom depends on the build.** With the fix removed: the assertions-enabled build aborts on `lfunc.c:218: poptbclist: Assertion 'tbc->tbclist.delta > 0' failed`; the release build **hangs forever**; the ASan build finishes with the wrong error, `attempt to call a nil value`. Note which way round that is -- the sanitizer build is the one that does *not* reproduce it, so the existing "clean under asan+ubsan" coverage was never going to find this.

The livelock, off a backtrace of the hang: `lua_resume -> precover -> unroll -> finishCcall -> finishpcallk (ldo.c:813) -> luaF_close -> prepcallclosemth (lfunc.c:161) -> callclosemethod -> luaG_callerror`. `finishpcallk` closes from the stack base, `__close` is called on a non-function, and that error re-enters `precover`, which closes from the base again.

**`old_errfunc` is not fixed and is a separate, much smaller gap.** A restored program's error is now correct but carries no traceback, because the record has no `old_errfunc` or `L->errfunc` and dv's message handler does not run at the throw point. 10.2 already calls that out of scope. Fixing it needs a format change, unlike this.

**Evidence.**

The shim reads and writes the field: `out->funcidx = ci->u2.funcidx;` (src/dshim.c:91) and `ci->u2.funcidx = f->funcidx;` (src/dshim.c:530). But the wire format has ten words per frame (`#define DS_FRAME_WORDS 10`) and funcidx is not one of them -- src/dsnap.c:1385-1394 writes exactly `is_c, has_k, func_index, top_index, pc, nextraargs, nresults, callstatus, ctx, is_vararg`. The decode side does `memset(out, 0, sizeof(*out));` (src/dsnap.c:1540) and never assigns `funcidx`, so every restored frame gets funcidx == 0. `callstatus` *is* carried verbatim (src/dsnap.c:1392) and CIST_YPCALL is in DSHIM_KNOWN_FLAGS (src/dshim.c:361), so the flag that makes the core read the field survives while the field does not. The reader is ldo.c:811, inside finishpcallk: `StkId func = restorestack(L, ci->u2.funcidx);` then `func = luaF_close(L, func, status, 1); luaD_seterrorobj(L, status, func);`. With `#define restorestack(L,n) cast(StkId, cast_charp(L->stack.p) + (n))` (src/ldo.h:46), funcidx == 0 means the stack base. Every parked agent carries a CIST_YPCALL frame: dtask.c:92 is `lua_pcallk(co, nargs, LUA_MULTRET, ef, 0, taskcont);` and lapi.c:1104 sets `ci->u2.funcidx = cast_int(savestack(L, c.func));` on that frame. Guest `pcall` frames are the same (lbaselib.c:480). No test resumes a restored thread into an error, which is why this is green: `a_thread_inside_pcall_round_trips` (test/dsnap_check.c:1992) only checks the success path ("the pcall still returns true").

**Failure scenario.**

Snapshot this agent parked on the wait, restore it, push a message and resume:
  CLOSED = false
  local q = queue.declare('inbox', {cap = 1})
  local function work()
    defer CLOSED = true
    local ok, err = pcall(function() queue.wait({q}); error('boom') end)
    return tostring(CLOSED)      -- 'false' on a fresh run
  end
Fresh run: the error unwinds to the guest pcall frame, whose funcidx names the anonymous function's slot, so `luaF_close` (lfunc.c:230-239, `while (L->tbclist.p >= level)`) closes only tbc slots above it; `work`'s deferred block has not run, and `work` returns 'false'. Restored run: funcidx is 0, level is the stack base, so the same call closes *every* tbc slot and *every* open upvalue in the thread -- `work`'s `defer` fires while its own scope is still live and is popped off tbclist, so it never fires again, and `work` returns 'true'. Any closure holding an open upvalue over a live local in an outer frame is silently detached at the same moment. Second, worse variant: a restored program with no tbc at all (empty tbclist rests exactly on `L->stack.p`, per src/dshim.c:139), so `L->tbclist.p >= level` is true for level == stack base and `poptbclist` runs on an empty list -- it reads the uninitialised `tbclist.delta` overlay of slot 0, leaves `L->tbclist.p` pointing below the stack allocation, and `prepcallclosemth` then writes the error object over `stack.p[1]`, which for a dv instance is the driver's own `taskbody` function slot, before trying to call `__close` on a nil.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Serialize funcidx as an eleventh frame word (bumping DS_FRAME_WORDS and DS_THREAD_VERSION), or reconstruct it in ds_buildthread for every frame with CIST_YPCALL set as `savestack` of the next frame's function slot -- the two agree by construction -- and validate it in diluvium_shim_checkframes the way func_index already is (a value of 0, or one not equal to the next frame's function, must be refused rather than restored).

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

Every quote is accurate at the cited line, and I could not find any check that blocks the failure.

Verified: dsnap.c:1385-1394 writes exactly ten frame words (DS_FRAME_WORDS 10, dsnap.c:1306) and funcidx is not among them; dsnap.c:1540 `memset(out, 0, sizeof(*out))` is followed by ten assignments (1541-1550) that never set `out->funcidx`, and ds_hook_finish (1610-1654) only marks tbc slots. Repo-wide grep shows funcidx is touched only in dshim.c:91 (read at capture), dshim.c:530 (`ci->u2.funcidx = f->funcidx;` at restore), lapi.c:1104, ldo.c:811, dshim.h:64, lstate.h. So every restored frame gets funcidx == 0, while `callstatus` (dsnap.c:1392) carries CIST_YPCALL verbatim and DSHIM_KNOWN_FLAGS (dshim.c:361-362) permits it.

Reachability traced to a caller: diluvium_shim_capturable/framecapturable (dshim.c:278-325) refuse only non-suspended, hooked, and continuation-less C frames — a YPCALL frame is capturable; diluvium_shim_checkframes (dshim.c:400-486) validates func_index, top_index, monotonicity, nresults, flag set, pc and varargs but never funcidx. And the case does not need a guest pcall at all: dv_run (dv.c:577-582) pushes taskbody + dv_msghandler + chunk and taskbody enters lua_pcallk (dtask.c:92), so every parked instance's outermost frame carries CIST_YPCALL with a live u2.funcidx; dv_restore (dv.c:760) adopts the wire-restored chain and dv_resume (dv.c:658) calls lua_resume on it. Any error raised by the woken program goes precover -> findpcall -> finishpcallk -> `StkId func = restorestack(L, ci->u2.funcidx);` (ldo.c:811) with restorestack being byte arithmetic off L->stack.p (ldo.h:46), so level is the stack base instead of slot 4 (slot 1 = base_ci nil, 2 = taskbody, 3 = msghandler, 4 = chunk closure) as in a fresh run.

Consequences confirmed in this tree: luaF_close (lfunc.c:230-239) closes every open upvalue and every tbc slot at/above the base; and with no tbc at all, lstate.c:167 sets tbclist.p = stack.p so `L->tbclist.p >= level` is true, entering poptbclist on an empty list — whose first line is `lua_assert(tbc->tbclist.delta > 0)` and which then reads the uninitialised `delta` padding of StackValue (lobject.h:148-154; prepstack only does setnilvalue2s). prepcallclosemth (lfunc.c:156-161) then does luaD_seterrorobj(L, status, level+1), writing the error object over slot 2 — taskbody's own function slot — and calls __close on the nil in slot 1.

Test coverage claim verified: a_thread_inside_pcall_round_trips asserts only "true:returned" (dsnap_check.c:2012-2013); an_agent_parked_on_wait_round_trips and dv_check.c:880-960 exercise only success paths; dv_check.c:129 errors a fresh instance, never a restored one. Nothing in test/ or script/ resumes a restored thread into an error.

Doc check: the only nearby sentence is §10.2 line 999 ("**It is out of scope for M6 anyway**, because restoring `old_errfunc` and the error-handler chain correctly is fiddly and nothing needs it yet"), but that is not a refusal and the code neither refuses nor honors it — §10.4 (line 1170), §14 (line 2035) and the shipped tests treat captured pcall frames as delivered, and the driver frame is a pcall frame on every parked agent. So this is not a limitation the doc names as not-done.

*Correction to the report:* Two refinements, neither of which weakens the finding. (1) The report frames this around a guest `pcall`; it is broader — dtask's driver frame carries CIST_YPCALL on every parked instance (dtask.c:92 via dv.c:577-582), so *any* error raised by *any* restored program after dv_resume hits it, with no guest pcall involved. (2) The predicted Lua output ('true' instead of 'false') is optimistic: after the over-close, tbclist.p lands back on stack.p, so `L->tbclist.p >= level` is true again and the run also takes the empty-list branch — ending in a bogus __close-on-nil error with slot 2 (taskbody) clobbered and L->top.p reset to 2, or a precover livelock when the uninitialised `delta` padding reads 0. Also minor: funcidx is a byte offset, not a slot index (savestack/restorestack are char arithmetic), which does not change the conclusion that 0 names the stack base. Separately noted but out of scope for this finding: `old_errfunc` is likewise absent from the ten frame words and restores as 0, and the thread record carries no `L->errfunc`, so dv's message handler does not run at the throw point in a restored thread.

---

<a id="f1"></a>

### 1. A woken instance has no instruction budget at all: the count hook is never re-armed

`src/dvs.c`:640 &middot; blocks-building-on-it &middot; correctness &middot; audit `m7`

**Status: open.** Profile C, behind the same switch.

**Evidence.**

dvs_wake: "/* The budget goes back on before the restore, for the same reason it goes on\n     before a load: 'dv_set_budget' refuses a running instance, and a woken agent\n     that had a budget must not come back without one. */\n  if (sl->instructions != 0 || sl->memory_kb != 0)\n    dv_set_budget(inst, sl->instructions, sl->memory_kb);" followed by dv_restore at 641. dv_set_budget only stores the number (src/dv.c:208 `inst->insn_limit = instructions;`). The count hook that enforces it is armed in exactly one place in the tree -- src/dv.c:571, inside dv_run: `lua_sethook(inst->co, dv_insn_hook, LUA_MASKCOUNT, DV_HOOK_STEP);`. `grep -n "sethook" src/dv.c src/dsnap.c` returns only that line and the disarm at dv.c:193. dv_restore (dv.c:731-781) adopts the thread the snapshot layer built and never calls lua_sethook, and dvs_wake never calls dv_run. So after a wake insn_limit != 0, no LUA_MASKCOUNT hook exists on the thread, dv_insn_hook never runs, inst->insn_used stays 0 and inst->exceeded is never set. (The memory cap survives, because it lives in the allocator installed by dv_new; only the instruction budget is lost.)

**Failure scenario.**

Spawn a child with instructions = 100000. It pushes {op = 'hibernate', wake_on_message = true} and parks; dvs_push wakes it on the next dvs_step. It then runs `while true do end`. Before the hibernate that loop died at 100k instructions with dv_exceeded() == 1 and an "exceeded" event; after the wake the hook is gone, so sw->host.drive() at dvs.c:1122 never returns and the whole swarm hangs. dvs_budget still reports 100000 and dv_usage reports 0 insns used forever. This is 9.4's stated purpose ("A guest cannot meaningfully limit itself, because a runaway loop never yields and nothing cooperative will stop it") defeated by item 6 of the same six-item list.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Arm the count hook on the restored thread -- either in dv_restore when inst->insn_limit != 0, or by exposing a dv_ call that dvs_wake makes after dv_restore. Carry insn_used across the snapshot too, otherwise a hibernate/wake cycle still resets consumption to zero and the budget is per-residency rather than per-instance. Test: budget a child, hibernate/wake it, then run an unbounded loop and assert dv_exceeded().

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I tried to refute this and could not. Every mechanical link checks out against this tree.

1. The quoted dvs_wake code exists verbatim, but at src/dvs.c:682-687, not 640 (line 640 is the closing brace of the malloc-failure branch in dvs_hibernate). Confirmed: `dv_set_budget(inst, sl->instructions, sl->memory_kb);` at 686, `if (dv_restore(inst, NULL, sl->snap, sl->snaplen) != DV_OK) {` at 687.

2. dv_set_budget (src/dv.c:200) only stores numbers: `inst->insn_limit = instructions;` at line 209 (not 208) and `inst->mem_limit = memory_kb * 1024u;` at 210. It arms nothing.

3. The count hook is armed in exactly one place: `lua_sethook(inst->co, dv_insn_hook, LUA_MASKCOUNT, DV_HOOK_STEP);` at src/dv.c:575 (not 571; 571 is the guarding `if`), inside dv_run. The only other lua_sethook in the dv/dvs/dsnap layer is the self-disarm at src/dv.c:193 inside dv_insn_hook. dv_restore (src/dv.c:735-787) and dv_resume (src/dv.c:608) never call it.

4. There is no host-side workaround, which the reporter did not even claim: dv_run's `if (inst->started) return DV_BUSY;` (src/dv.c:559) plus dv_restore setting `inst->started = 1` means a woken instance can never re-enter dv_run, and dv.h exposes no hook-arming call (grep for "hook" in src/dv.h finds only two comments, no function).

5. The hook cannot survive the snapshot either. The restored thread is made by `co = lua_newthread(L);` at src/dsnap.c:1486, and lua_newthread inherits hook state from the creating thread (src/lstate.c:290-292: `L1->hookmask = L->hookmask; L1->basehookcount = L->basehookcount; L1->hook = L->hook;`). The creating thread is inst->L, which never carries a count hook -- dv_run deliberately arms inst->co instead, as its own comment at src/dv.c:572-574 says. And no hook state is serialized: grep for `hookmask`/`L->hook` across dsnap.c, dshim.c, dv.c, dvs.c returns nothing (dsnap.c's `ds_hook_*` are codec callbacks).

6. No swarm-side fallback enforcement exists. dvs.c's only budget-facing reads are dvs_budget (reports sl->instructions verbatim, dvs.c:261), dv_usage in do_query (dvs.c:1026, reporting only), and dv_exceeded in dvs_step (dvs.c:1175, reading a flag only dv_insn_hook ever sets, src/dv.c:192).

7. The scenario is reachable entirely from guest ops: do_spawn reads the `instructions` field (dvs.c:892) and records it (dvs.c:373-374); a spawn arms the hook because dvs_spawn budgets before load and the host drives with dv_run; do_hibernate (dvs.c:962) honours `wake_on_message` from the request (dvs.c:992-993) and calls dvs_hibernate; dvs_push buffers into sl->pend; dvs_step wakes at dvs.c:1137 before `keep = sw->host.drive(...)` at dvs.c:1168 (the reporter said 1122). After the wake the host can only drive with dv_resume, which resumes a hookless thread.

8. The asymmetry is proven by the project's own test: test/dv_check.c:1122-1147 shows `"local n = 0 while true do n = n + 1 end"` under a 200000 budget stopping with DV_ERROR and dv_exceeded() true -- but only via dv_run. No test wakes a budgeted instance and asserts enforcement: test/dvs_check.c:869-889 is the only hibernate+budget test and its comment "The budget survives hibernation" is about readback only -- it asserts `dvs_budget(sw, root, &insns, NULL) == DVS_OK && insns == 5000000` while cached and never wakes it. That test is exactly the "green for the wrong reason" shape convention 2 warns about: it makes the budget look preserved when only the number is.

9. The doc does not disclose the limitation anywhere. I read all of 9.4 (doc/Messaging.md:915-929), 9.5, 10.7 (the "checked at hibernate time" list, 1330-1375), 10.8 ("Queue handles do not survive." -- the one thing named as not surviving), and the M7 record. §9.4 states "A guest cannot meaningfully limit itself, because a runaway loop never yields and nothing cooperative will stop it." with the table row "| Instruction budget | `lua_sethook` count hook, public API |", and doc/Messaging.md:2147-2154 says "**M7: swarm layer** -- done. Instance table, `system/lifecycle` and `system/events`, attenuation, budgets, orphan policy, rate limits, snapshot cache with `wake_on_message`. ... **All met.**" Section 17 mentions budgets only twice, both about the missing getter (2296-2297, 2622-2623). Nothing states that instruction enforcement ends at a hibernate.

So: after a wake, insn_limit is non-zero and readable via dvs_budget, no LUA_MASKCOUNT hook exists on the thread, dv_insn_hook never runs, insn_used stays 0 and exceeded stays 0 forever, and an unbounded loop in a woken agent hangs the drive call. The memory cap does survive because dv_alloc consults inst->mem_limit and the allocator is installed in dv_new. The secondary point is also correct: insn_used is not carried through the snapshot, so even with a re-arm the budget would be per-residency.

*Correction to the report:* Locations in the report are off. Correct anchors: the budget-then-restore pair is src/dvs.c:682-687 (dv_set_budget at 686, dv_restore at 687), not dvs.c:640. The store is src/dv.c:209, not 208. The sole arming is src/dv.c:575 (the `if` is at 571). dv_restore spans src/dv.c:735-787, not 731-781. The drive call is src/dvs.c:1168, not 1122. Two facts to add that strengthen it: (a) there is no host-side workaround, because dv_run refuses a restored instance at src/dv.c:559 (`if (inst->started) return DV_BUSY;`) and dv.h exposes no hook-arming call, so the fix must be inside dv_restore or a new dv_ entry point; (b) the hook could not survive the snapshot even in principle -- the restored thread is `lua_newthread(L)` at src/dsnap.c:1486 and src/lstate.c:290-292 inherits the hook from inst->L, which never has one. Also worth flagging alongside: test/dvs_check.c:869-889 is a test that reads "The budget survives hibernation" but only asserts the number reads back while cached, so it currently certifies the wrong half of the claim.

<a id="f2"></a>

### 2. A spawn request in the documented shape gives the child no budget at all

`src/dvs.c`:846 &middot; blocks-building-on-it &middot; false-doc-claim &middot; audit `m7`

**Status: fixed.** `field_budget` reads the nested form 9.1 documents and still accepts the flat one. `a_documented_budget_reaches_the_child`.

**Evidence.**

doc/Messaging.md:812 documents the one and only spawn request shape as `  budget = { instructions = 5e6, memory_kb = 512 },` (grep for `op *= *"` finds exactly one request in the document). do_spawn reads "field_int(msg, len, \"instructions\", &insns);\n  field_int(msg, len, \"memory_kb\", &mem);" -- top-level keys. field_int opens a fresh cursor (dvs.c:744) and diluvium_mp_field scans only the keys of the outer map, mp_skip-ing each value whole (src/dmsgpack.c:2074-2091), so it never descends into `budget`. dvs.c:748-749 even cites the example ("A budget written as 5e6 arrives as a float, which is what 9.1's own example shows") -- the float was read from that line and the nesting was not.

**Failure scenario.**

A supervisor pushes exactly the request in 9.1: insns and mem stay 0, build() skips dv_set_budget entirely (dvs.c:351), and the child runs with no instruction limit and no memory limit. There is no "denied" event and no error -- the spawn reports success, so nothing tells the author the budget was discarded. Every spawn request in test/dvs_check.c is {op='spawn', code=..., caps=...} with no budget, so no test covers the path; the only budget assertions (dvs_check.c:840-849) go through dvs_root's C arguments.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Read `budget` as a nested map (descend with the cursor after diluvium_mp_field(&c, "budget")), keeping the flat keys if you want both, or correct 9.1's example. Either way add a test that spawns through system/lifecycle with a budget and asserts dvs_budget on the child returns it.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

Confirmed from source in the audit tree, though the reporter's line anchors are wrong (they read the stale worktree copy .claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-8/src/dvs.c, a uniform -46 offset; the quoted code text itself is accurate).

Doc, verbatim, doc/Messaging.md:809-815 — the document's one and only spawn request:
  queue.push(sys, {
    op    = "spawn",
    code  = handler_code_ref,
    caps  = { "queue:work/*", "queue:system/events" },
    budget = { instructions = 5e6, memory_kb = 512 },
    wake_on_message = true,
  })
followed at :818 by "The swarm layer drains that queue and acts on it, calling `dv_new` on the instance ABI."

Code, verbatim, src/dvs.c:892-893 (in do_spawn, which begins at :853):
    field_int(msg, len, "instructions", &insns);
    field_int(msg, len, "memory_kb", &mem);
field_int (src/dvs.c:786-802) does diluvium_mp_open(&c, msg, len) then diluvium_mp_field(&c, key). diluvium_mp_field (src/dmsgpack.c:2100-2117) reads the outer map header and, on each non-matching key, does "if (!diluvium_mp_skip(c)) return 0;" — it walks only the outer map's keys and never descends into a nested value. test/dvs_check.c:214-217 asserts precisely that property ("a field found past a nested table, so 'skip' descends"). So both keys are absent, insns and mem stay 0.

Failure scenario traced end to end: drain -> do_spawn (src/dvs.c:1090) -> build (src/dvs.c:350). build guards the budget with "if (req->instructions != 0 || req->memory_kb != 0)" (src/dvs.c:362), so dv_set_budget is never called; insn_limit and mem_limit remain 0; the instruction hook is only installed under "if (inst->insn_limit != 0)" (src/dv.c:571) and both enforcement guards (src/dv.c:191 and src/dv.c:152) test != 0. The child therefore runs with no instruction limit and no memory limit, and do_spawn emits "spawned" (src/dvs.c:920) — no denial, no error, so nothing tells the author the budget was discarded.

Refutation attempts, all failed:
- Nothing flattens the request anywhere. The literal string "budget" occurs exactly once in the whole repository — doc/Messaging.md:813 — and never in src/, test/, script/ or bindings/. No binding wraps the lifecycle protocol.
- src/dvs.h documents no on-wire field names at all (its lifecycle text at :166-167 only shows {op = "hibernate"}), so 9.1 is the sole specification of the request shape, and four of its five keys (op, code, caps, wake_on_message) ARE read literally by do_spawn — only budget is nested, so a reader has every reason to believe it works.
- The surrounding sections do not state the limitation: 9.2's "As built" block, 9.4 "Resource control", 9.5's two corrections, and the M7 record (doc/Messaging.md:2148-2153: "Instance table, `system/lifecycle` and `system/events`, attenuation, budgets, orphan policy, rate limits ... **All met.**") say nothing about the budget key being unread. All of section 17 (doc/Messaging.md:2439-2700) was read; its only budget-related entry is the dvs_spawn/dvs_budget accessor gap (:2622-2623), a different subject.
- No test covers it: every spawn request in test/dvs_check.c (lines 243, 360, 422, 466, 470, 519, 550, 733, 923) omits budget, and "instructions"/"memory_kb" appear nowhere in that file as message keys; the only dvs_budget assertions (test/dvs_check.c:846-853, 887) reach the slot through dvs_root's C arguments, which is a path field_int is not on.

*Correction to the report:* Substance stands; the anchors need fixing. Correct location is src/dvs.c:892-893 (do_spawn begins at src/dvs.c:853), field_int is src/dvs.c:786-802 with its fresh cursor at src/dvs.c:790 and the "5e6 arrives as a float" comment at src/dvs.c:794-795, and diluvium_mp_field is src/dmsgpack.c:2100-2117 (the reporter's 2074-2091 is inside diluvium_mp_skip's comment). The reporter's numbers come from .claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-8, not from /home/user/diluvium. Also worth adding to the report: the same doc block's `code = handler_code_ref` is read as a plain string (field_str into char code[8192] at src/dvs.c:856, 872), and `queue.lookup` in the example is contradicted by 9.2's own "As built" note that both system queues are declared by the guest — but neither of those is part of this finding.

<a id="f3"></a>

### 3. doc says a stray lua.h in the swarm layer fails the build; dvs.c already includes lua.h transitively and both targets put the whole core on the include path

`doc/Messaging.md`:2158 &middot; blocks-building-on-it &middot; false-doc-claim &middot; audit `single-point`

**Status: fixed.** Doc corrected. The symbol check at link time is the real guarantee, and it now runs in CI.

**Evidence.**

doc/Messaging.md:2155-2160 verbatim: "`src/dvs.h` and `src/dvs.c` — 11.0 KB, a separate library (`libdiluvium-swarm`, prefix `dvs_`) that is not linked into the runtime. It includes `dv.h` and the codec's token cursor and nothing else from the tree; the Makefile compiles it apart from the amalgamation so a stray `lua.h` fails the build rather than passing unnoticed. That is 4.1's boundary made checkable instead of merely stated: the moment this file needs a `lua_State`, something has been put in the wrong layer." Makefile:316-318 repeats it ("a stray include of lua.h would fail here") and Makefile:407-408 repeats it again ("Compiling without lua.h shows the layer boundary holds for the *preprocessor*"). The code: src/dvs.c:26 is `#include "dmsgpack.h"`, and src/dmsgpack.h:22 is `#include "lua.h"` — unconditional, inside the plain `#ifndef dmsgpack_h` guard. Both compile lines pass the entire core on -I: Makefile:320 `gcc $(TEST_CFLAGS) -DMAKE_LIB -I$(CURDIR)/.data` and Makefile:413 `gcc -O2 -fPIC -c -I$(CURDIR)/.data`, where .data is `cp -r $(CURDIR)/src/*` (Makefile:70) and therefore contains lua.h, lstate.h, lobject.h and every other core header (`ls .data/lua.h .data/lstate.h` confirms both).

**Failure scenario.**

dvs.c is not compiled without lua.h; it is compiled with lua.h already included and with lstate.h one `#include` away. Adding `#include "lstate.h"` to src/dvs.c and reading `L->ci->callstatus` compiles cleanly under both `make dvs_check` and `make build_swarm_lib`. The only surviving check is the `nm -u | grep -E '^_?lua'` at Makefile:415, which sees undefined *functions* and nothing else — so any dependency on a core type, macro, or struct layout enters the "separate optional library" undetected. That is precisely the leak 4.1 exists to prevent, and M8 packaging is planned on the strength of the claim being enforced.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Either make it true — give dvs.c a codec view that does not drag lua.h in (split the token-cursor declarations out of dmsgpack.h) and compile it with `-I` pointing at a directory holding only dv.h and that view — or correct doc/Messaging.md:2157-2159 and the two Makefile comments to say the boundary is checked at the linker only, for functions only.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I tried to refute this and could not. Every quote is accurate and at the cited line.

DOC QUOTE VERIFIED. doc/Messaging.md:2155-2160, verbatim, line numbers confirmed by numbered read:
  2157  codec's token cursor and nothing else from the tree; the Makefile compiles it apart
  2158  from the amalgamation so a stray `lua.h` fails the build rather than passing
  2159  unnoticed. That is 4.1's boundary made checkable instead of merely stated: the
  2160  moment this file needs a `lua_State`, something has been put in the wrong layer.

CODE QUOTES VERIFIED. src/dvs.c:26 is `#include "dmsgpack.h"` (lines 21-27 are stdarg/stdio/stdlib/string, then dmsgpack.h, then dvs.h; nothing else). src/dmsgpack.h:22 is `#include "lua.h"`, unconditional, sitting at the top of the plain `#ifndef dmsgpack_h` guard opened at line 16 — I read lines 14-26 to rule out a conditional or a `#define lua_h` stub. I grepped src/dvs.c and src/dvs.h for `lua_State|lua_|LUA_`: the only hits are the two prose comments (src/dvs.c:7, src/dvs.h:35) asserting the boundary. Makefile:70 `cp -r $(CURDIR)/src/* $(CURDIR)/.data`; Makefile:320 and Makefile:413 both pass `-I$(CURDIR)/.data`; src/lua.h and src/lstate.h both exist (ls confirms), so both are in .data.

THE REFUTATION THAT DOESN'T WORK. I looked for a reading of 2158 under which it is true. The only coherent reading of "a stray `lua.h` fails the build" is "an accidental `#include "lua.h"` in dvs.c would be a compile error." That is false, and it is false without any hypothetical: lua.h is *already* in dvs.c's translation unit today via dvs.c:26 -> dmsgpack.h:22. Both targets that compile dvs.c are green in CI (.github/workflows/test.yml:156 `make dvs_check`, :186 `make build_swarm_lib`) and the doc itself reports M7 done with 66 checks passing. A compilation in which lua.h is already successfully included cannot be one where a stray lua.h fails. No compile needed to establish this, which matters given the no-build constraint.

Worse than the reporter noticed: because both targets compile `.data/dvs.c` and lua.h sits in `.data` next to it, `#include "lua.h"` resolves through the quote-include rule (directory of the including file) even before `-I` is consulted. There is no configuration in which the claimed failure occurs.

I checked for a check the reporter might have missed. Grepped Makefile, script/, test/, bindings/ and .github/ for lua.h and for dvs: the only enforcement is Makefile:415-422, `nm -u ... | grep -E '^_?lua'`, which inspects undefined *symbols* in dvs.o. Struct field access, macros and typedefs emit no symbol, so `#include "lstate.h"` plus `L->ci->callstatus` passes it. lstate.h is self-sufficient here — it includes lua.h, lobject.h, ltm.h, lzio.h (all in .data) with no LUA_CORE guard and no #error in it or llimits.h — so nothing upstream blocks that include either. And note that even a direct `lua_*` *function* call escapes `make dvs_check` entirely, because Makefile:322-323 links `.data/onelua.c`, the whole amalgamated core; only build_swarm_lib's nm check would see it.

I also read the whole M7 section (doc 2140-2175), 4.1 (165-185) and 11.5 (1589-1600) looking for the limitation stated a few sentences later, which is the usual way these claims are rescued. It is not there. Grepping the doc for `nm -u|link time|linker|undefined symbol` returns nothing — the doc never scopes the boundary check to the linker, and 2159 makes the opposite claim ("made checkable instead of merely stated").

SCOPE CORRECTION to the finding, which I'd have the parent carry forward. The doc's first clause is accurate and should not be flagged: "It includes `dv.h` and the codec's token cursor and nothing else from the tree" is a true statement about dvs.c's direct includes (dmsgpack.h is the token cursor; dvs.h:44 is `#include "dv.h"`). The falsehood is confined to the enforcement clause at 2157-2160. Contrary to the finding's framing, the two Makefile comments are not equally wrong: Makefile:407-410 does honestly separate preprocessor from linker and calls the symbol check "the claim actually being made", and .github/workflows/test.yml:184 goes further, calling it "a stronger one than compiling without lua.h" — so the intent is already recorded correctly in the build files. What is wrong there is the factual premise "Compiling without lua.h" (Makefile:407, test.yml:184), since the compile is not without lua.h; plus Makefile:316-317 "dvs.c sees dv.h and dmsgpack.h and nothing else, and a stray include of lua.h would fail here", and test.yml:151-153 "dvs.c includes dv.h and nothing else from the tree ... The build is the check for that" — that last one is wrong twice over, since dvs.c also includes dmsgpack.h, which the doc itself gets right.

This meets criterion (b): a verbatim doc sentence the code contradicts, with exact locations. It matters under convention 3 rather than as a runtime bug — 2159 tells a reader that 4.1's boundary is mechanically enforced when only the function-symbol half is, and M8 packaging is planned on that.

*Correction to the report:* Confirmed as a false-doc-claim, with its scope narrowed.

What is false: doc/Messaging.md:2157-2160, "the Makefile compiles it apart from the amalgamation so a stray `lua.h` fails the build rather than passing unnoticed. That is 4.1's boundary made checkable instead of merely stated." No such build-time check exists. src/dvs.c:26 includes dmsgpack.h, whose line 22 is an unconditional `#include "lua.h"`, so lua.h is already in dvs.c's translation unit in both targets that compile it (Makefile:320, Makefile:413). Additionally .data holds a copy of all of src/ (Makefile:70) and is passed on -I, and .data/dvs.c sits beside .data/lua.h, so a direct include resolves by the quote-include rule regardless of -I. The only real enforcement is Makefile:415-422's `nm -u | grep -E '^_?lua'`, which sees undefined functions only: a dependency on a core type, macro or struct layout (`#include "lstate.h"`; `L->ci->callstatus`) passes it, and under `make dvs_check` even a `lua_*` call links, because Makefile:322-323 links the whole amalgamated `onelua.c`.

What is NOT false, and should not be reported: the preceding clause "It includes `dv.h` and the codec's token cursor and nothing else from the tree" is accurate about dvs.c's direct includes.

Suggested doc text for 2157-2160: "...the Makefile compiles it apart from the amalgamation and checks the boundary at the linker: `make build_swarm_lib` fails if dvs.o has any undefined `lua*` symbol. That covers functions only. lua.h is in fact reachable in this translation unit — dmsgpack.h includes it — so a dependency on a core type, macro or struct layout would compile and link unnoticed. 4.1's boundary is enforced for calls and stated for everything else."

The two Makefile comments and the CI comments need the narrower fix of dropping the false premise: Makefile:316-317 ("dvs.c sees dv.h and dmsgpack.h and nothing else, and a stray include of lua.h would fail here"), Makefile:407 and .github/workflows/test.yml:184 ("Compiling without lua.h"), and test.yml:151-153 ("dvs.c includes dv.h and nothing else from the tree ... The build is the check for that"). Makefile:407-410's preprocessor/linker distinction and its "which is the claim actually being made" are otherwise the honest version already.

If the project would rather make the claim true than retract it: split the token-cursor declarations out of dmsgpack.h into a header with no lua.h, and compile dvs.c with -I pointing at a directory containing only dv.h and that header — then a stray core include really does fail, and the sentence at 2159 earns its "checkable".

<a id="f4"></a>

### 4. Endpoint references can still be forged: a queue round-trip launders a guest-decoded ext 0x02

`src/dmsgpack.c`:835 &middot; blocks-building-on-it &middot; correctness &middot; audit `doc-claims`

**Status: fixed.** Closed at the encoder: a reserved ext code cannot be written, so the bytes never leave the state that made them. `the_laundering_route_is_closed`.

**Evidence.**

doc/Messaging.md:1880 says '**"A reference cannot be forged" was false when written, and is now true.**' and :1894-1897 says 'only the host's reach the resolver: the queue delivery path and snapshot restore are trusted, guest-callable `msgpack.decode` is not. A guest decoding ext 0x02 gets the opaque ext wrapper an embedder without a resolver has always got, which is a documented and inert value.' The wrapper is not inert. mp_decode_ext returns it with the *original code* kept at [3] (dmsgpack.c:1121 `if (!c->trusted) { mp_ext_push(L, code, data, len); return; }`), and the plain encoder re-emits that code verbatim: dmsgpack.c:835 `if (kind == MP_SHAPE_EXT) {` ... `mp_enc_ext(ctx->buf, code, data, len);` with no range check (only mp_ext_new at :550 enforces 0x10-0x7F). queue.push encodes with diluvium_msgpack_encode, and queue.pop/dq_take decode with diluvium_msgpack_decode, which sets `c.trusted = 1` (dmsgpack.c:1383; called at dqueue.c:642 and dqueue.c:671). So one push+pop turns a guest-minted ext 0x02 into a resolver-produced reference. Run on dist/diluvium_debug: `local w=msgpack.decode("\xc7\x06\x02peer-b"); local q=queue.declare('l'); queue.push(q,w); local ref=queue.pop(q)` gives `getmetatable(ref) == false` (the private de_ref_mt) and `msgpack.encode(w)` is byte-identical to the input, so the name is preserved.

**Failure scenario.**

Against dv_check.c's own host setup (`dv_endpoint_allow(inst, "9", 1, 42)` at dv_check.c:726), a guest runs: w = msgpack.decode('\xd4\x029'); q = queue.declare('l'); queue.push(q, w); ref = queue.pop(q); endpoint.bind(ref, 'peer'). de_bind's de_ref_bytes accepts it (dendpoint.c:198) and de_allowed returns token 42 *before* any host callback (dendpoint.c:210), so the program binds and pushes to a peer the host never handed it — the exact end-to-end exploit §17 (doc:2638-2650) says is now closed. Measured here: after the laundering, endpoint.bind's refusal changes from 'the first argument is not an endpoint reference' to 'this host binds no endpoints', i.e. it is accepted as a genuine reference. Both guards stay green because they only try the direct decode: test_endpoint.lua's forgery loop (which lists the very bytes '\xc7\x06\x02peer-b') and dv_check.c:714 a_guest_cannot_mint_a_reference.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Refuse a reserved ext code in the encoder's MP_SHAPE_EXT branch (dmsgpack.c:835-845) — a shape wrapper can only legitimately carry 0x10-0x7F, which is what mp_ext_new already enforces on construction. Then add the push/pop laundering vector to test_endpoint.lua's forgery list and to a_guest_cannot_mint_a_reference, since neither exercises a trusted decode of guest-supplied bytes.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

Every quote checks out at the stated line, the path is reachable, and I reproduced the exploit on the in-tree binary without compiling anything.

Code verified: src/dmsgpack.c:835 is `if (kind == MP_SHAPE_EXT) {`, and 839-844 read `[3]` into `code` and call `mp_enc_ext(ctx->buf, code, data, len);`. mp_enc_ext (308-323) emits `(unsigned char)code` with no range check; the 0x10-0x7F check exists only in mp_ext_new at 550-552, which guards construction. src/dmsgpack.c:1121-1124 is `if (!c->trusted) { mp_ext_push(L, code, data, len); return; }`, and mp_ext_push (567-577) keeps the original code at [3] under mp_shape_mt — precisely the shape the encoder at 835 honors. trusted=0 at 1342 (guest msgpack.decode) vs trusted=1 at 1383 (diluvium_msgpack_decode_n). dq_push calls diluvium_msgpack_encode at dqueue.c:553 after checking only gone/disabled/capacity, filtering nothing; dq_pop (642) and dq_take (671) both call diluvium_msgpack_decode, i.e. trusted=1.

Reproduced on dist/diluvium_debug (built 17:42, newer than dmsgpack.c 17:33, dqueue.c 08:51, dendpoint.c 05:40; running an existing binary, no make/gcc, no writes):
  w = msgpack.decode("\xd4\x029") -> table with a VISIBLE metatable (mp_shape_mt at 481-490 sets no __metatable)
  msgpack.encode(w) == "\xd4\x029" -> true (code 0x02 re-emitted verbatim)
  queue.push(q, w) -> true, "ok"; queue.pop(q) -> table with getmetatable == false, i.e. the private DE_REF_MT whose __metatable is false (dendpoint.c:132-133)
  endpoint.bind(popped, "peer") -> "this host binds no endpoints..." (dendpoint.c:218), so de_ref_bytes ACCEPTED it and it passed the ref==NULL guard at 206-210
  endpoint.bind(w, "peer") -> "the first argument is not an endpoint reference"
The error-message change is proof the laundered value is a genuine reference, not a lookalike.

End-to-end against dv_check.c's own host setup: with dv_endpoint_allow(inst, "9", 1, 42) at dv_check.c:726, de_allowed (dendpoint.c:69-79, called at 214) returns 42 for the laundered bytes, so de_bind skips the host callback entirely (215-230) and declares the endpoint queue registered under token 42 (241-247) — the same end state as endpoint_preauthorised at dv_check.c:640-647, which the host drains to the peer.

Doc quotes are verbatim and contradicted. doc/Messaging.md:1880 `**"A reference cannot be forged" was false when written, and is now true.**` is false. doc:1896-1897 "A guest decoding ext 0x02 gets the opaque ext wrapper an embedder without a resolver has always got, which is a documented and inert value." — the wrapper is not inert; it re-encodes to the identical bytes and one queue round-trip converts it into a resolver-produced reference. §17's most-serious correction (doc:2638-2650) claims this exploit is closed.

Refutations I tried and that failed: (a) no earlier check blocks it — mp_shape_of (498-512) compares the metatable by identity and mp_ext_push installs that exact metatable; (b) the doc does not state the limitation later — I read 1855-1915 in full and grepped the whole doc for trusted/untrusted/ext 0x02/inert/forge/launder/round-trip, finding no acknowledgement; (c) no test covers it — test/test_endpoint.lua:63-65 and test/dv_check.c:714-720 bind only the direct decode result, and grepping all of test/, script/, bindings/ for msgpack.decode plus queue.push turns up no test that pushes a decoded value through a queue; (d) the proposed fix does not break the legitimate relay, because a real reference is a DE_REF_MT table carrying no MP_SHAPE_EXT marker and de_resolver.encode is NULL (dendpoint.c:182-184), leaving the encoder's resolver hook at 864-871 unreachable for it — the shape branch at 835 is the only guest route to an ext byte, and only mp_ext_push can place a reserved code in a wrapper.

Two minor location errors in the report, immaterial to the claim: de_ref_bytes is called at dendpoint.c:200 (198 is de_bind's own line) and de_allowed at 214 (not 210). The primary location, dmsgpack.c:835, is exact.

*Correction to the report:* Finding stands as reported. Corrected line references: the de_ref_bytes call is dendpoint.c:200 (dendpoint.c:198 is de_bind's declaration) and de_allowed is dendpoint.c:214 (not :210); the untrusted-decode guard spans dmsgpack.c:1121-1124. The primary location dmsgpack.c:835 and all other cited lines (550, 1342, 1383, dqueue.c:553/642/671, dv_check.c:714/726) are accurate.

---

<a id="f5"></a>

### 5. The §10.10 snapshot field-validation layer has zero effective coverage: the fuzzer named as its acceptance criterion never reaches it, because it mutates the payload without recomputing the header digest

`script/fuzz_snapshot.py`:98 &middot; blocks-building-on-it &middot; test-passes-for-wrong-reason &middot; audit `wrong-reason`

**Status: open.** Profile C. The digest refuses every mutant before the field checks run.

**Evidence.**

The fuzzer's own docstring at script/fuzz_snapshot.py:97 says of the 32-bit field pokes: "# reaches diluvium_shim_checkframes." It does not. It mutates bytes inside the ext 0x08 thread record but never recomputes the header's SHA-256 payload digest, so every mutant dies in diluvium_snap_checkheader (src/dsnap.c:520-521, `if (strcmp(hex, want) != 0) rc = DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT;`) before ds_loadbody ever calls the validator.

MEASURED, all 429 mutants, by capturing each refusal string:
  398 refused "the payload is not the bytes the header was written for..." (DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT)
   11 refused "the bytes do not begin with a snapshot header" (truncated past the header)
   20 accepted -- and all 20 are BYTE-IDENTICAL to the reference (the poison value already equalled the field). Non-mutations, not benign mutations.
  0 of 429 reach diluvium_shim_checkframes.

MUTATION (whole validator removed). Inserted at src/dshim.c:412, top of diluvium_shim_checkframes' frame loop:
    cap = diluvium_shim_stackcapacity(co);
  +  return DILUVIUM_RES_OK;  /* MUTATION */
    for (i = 0; i < n; i++) {
Everything stays green:
  dshim_check   87 checks, 0 failed
  dsnap_check  142 checks, 0 failed
  dv_check     150 checks, 0 failed
  dvs_check     81 checks, 0 failed
  snap_fuzz    429 mutants: 409 refused, 20 accepted, 0 crashed, 0 timed out  (byte-identical to baseline)
  test_ci      passed 47   failed 0   skipped 3
  sanitize_checks  all contract tests clean under asan+ubsan
All 17 individual guards were also disabled one at a time (nslots, func_index, top_index, monotonic, nresults range, callstatus flags, is_c/CIST_C agreement, nresults/callstatus agreement, frame-is-a-function, C-frame-not-Lclosure, innermost-only-lacks-k, Lua-frame-is-Lclosure, pc in code array, is_vararg, nextraargs, maxstacksize room, setslot bound): every one UNCAUGHT by dshim_check, dsnap_check and snap_fuzz.

CONTROL (mutations do reach the binary): making checkframes always return DILUVIUM_RES_STACK aborts the fuzzer with "the unmutated snapshot does not restore (refused: ... a stack size outside what was allocated) -- every mutant would then be refused for the wrong reason".

THE FIELD LAYER IS LOAD-BEARING, just unobservable. With the digest disabled (`if (0 && strcmp(hex, want) != 0)` at src/dsnap.c:520): 335 refused, 92 accepted, 2 CRASHED (signal 6). Additionally disabling the two callstatus-consistency checks moves 15 more mutants from refused to accepted: 320 refused, 107 accepted. So those checks really do refuse 15 mutants -- but only when the digest is not masking them.

No test anywhere references checkframes: `grep -rn "checkframes|RES_PC|RES_CALLSTATUS|RES_NOT_FUNCTION|RES_NO_CONTINUATION|RES_NOT_MONOTONIC" test/` returns nothing.

DOC CONTRADICTED, verbatim. doc/Messaging.md:1437: "A structure-aware fuzzer is an acceptance criterion, not follow-up". doc/Messaging.md:2366-2368: "**Snapshot validation** (`script/fuzz_snapshot.py`, `make snap_fuzz`). Structure-aware mutation of a snapshot; assert refusal, never a crash. Plus a foreign host stamp refused. 430 mutants, 0 crashes." doc/Messaging.md:2535: "Measured against the fuzzer: 4 crashes with neither, 3 with field checks alone, 0 with both." -- the "0 with both" is today produced by the digest alone; the second layer contributes nothing the fuzzer can see. script/fuzz_snapshot.py:20-22: "a mutant that restores and runs is also fine, because some mutations are benign and some fields have wide legal ranges" -- the 20 accepted are not benign mutations, they are non-mutations.

**Failure scenario.**

Any regression in diluvium_shim_checkframes ships silently: deleting the entire function body passes all six contract suites, the 47-test Lua suite, the sanitizer sweep and the fuzzer with byte-identical output. Concretely, if the payload digest is ever weakened, made optional, or skipped for a trusted-source path (dsnap.c:513-515 already skips it when the header's `payload` field is empty, and src/dmsgpack.c:1658 already marks snapshot restore as `trusted`), the fuzzer's measured result changes from 0 crashes to 2 crashes (signal 6) with the field checks still present -- and nothing in the tree would have warned that the second layer had stopped being exercised. The doc's "430 mutants, 0 crashes" is evidence for the digest only.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

In script/fuzz_snapshot.py, after building each mutant, recompute the header's payload digest over the mutated payload and rewrite it, so mutants reach ds_loadbody and diluvium_shim_checkframes. Emit the tally by refusal code and fail the run if the count of mutants refused by the field layer is zero -- the same self-check already applied to the unmutated reference at fuzz_snapshot.py:174-180. Keep a second pass with the digest intact to cover integrity. Additionally give diluvium_shim_checkframes direct unit tests in dshim_check.c: it takes a plain diluvium_frame array, so each guard is callable without constructing a snapshot at all, exactly as the_exemption_is_narrow already does for diluvium_shim_framecapturable.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=likely.**

I tried to refute this and could not refute its core, which is verifiable by reading alone.

VERIFIED QUOTES AND LINES.
- src/dsnap.c:512-521 is as quoted: `if (rc == DILUVIUM_SNAP_ACCEPT && used != NULL && *used < len) {` / `if (lua_getfield(L, hdr, "payload") == LUA_TSTRING) {` ... `if (strcmp(hex, want) != 0)` / `rc = DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT;`.
- script/fuzz_snapshot.py:97-98 reads `# Every 32-bit field of the thread record, poisoned. This is the part that` / `# reaches diluvium_shim_checkframes.` (the reporter cited 98 and 97; both are right).
- src/dshim.c:401 is `LUA_API int diluvium_shim_checkframes (lua_State *co,` and `cap = diluvium_shim_stackcapacity(co);` immediately precedes the frame loop, so the claimed mutation point is real.

REACHABILITY, TRACED.
Only one caller exists: `grep -rn checkframes src/` returns src/dsnap.c:1573 plus declarations in dshim.h/dsnap.h. That call sits inside the thread decode hook, which runs inside `ds_loadbody` (src/dsnap.c:1756), which `diluvium_snap_load` reaches only after `diluvium_snap_checkheader` accepts (src/dsnap.c:1784 then 1795). test/snap_harness.c:130 calls `dv_restore`, and src/dv.c:752 calls `diluvium_snap_load` with `&opts`, `used` non-NULL. Real snapshots always carry a non-empty digest: src/dsnap.c:1741 calls `diluvium_snap_headerfor(L, opts, g, glen, ...)` and lines 1745-1747 emit header-then-payload, so the SHA-256 covers every payload byte.

Every mutation the fuzzer emits lands at offset >= `at`, the start of the ext-0x08 payload (fuzz_snapshot.py:108 `off = at + 1 + w*4`, 114 `m[at] = v`, 122 `blob[:at+1+w*4]`, 125 `blob[:cut]`, 131-132 flips from `at` onward) — i.e. inside the digest-covered region. So any mutant that actually differs from the reference either fails `strcmp(hex, want)` (CORRUPT) or truncates into the header (BAD_HEADER). Conversely an *accepted* mutant must have matched the digest, which for header-unchanged bytes means byte-identical to the reference. The reporter's deduction that the 20 accepted are non-mutations therefore follows from source, not just from their run.

COVERAGE, CHECKED MYSELF, BY BEHAVIOUR NOT NAME.
`grep -rn "checkframes|resreason|DILUVIUM_RES_|neededstack" test/ script/ bindings/ Makefile doc/` hits only fuzz_snapshot.py:17 and :98. test/dshim_check.c exercises the *capture* side (`diluvium_shim_framecapturable`, lines 892-919, `the_exemption_is_narrow`), never the restore-side validator. test/dsnap_check.c's thread cases (1758-2200) all round-trip through the raw graph codec (helper at 129-132, `encode_graph`/`decode_graph`, no header, no digest) and none tampers with the encoded thread record — its tampering helpers (`tamper` at 839, the prototype cases at 1547/1590) touch header fields and inlined prototype bytes only. test/dv_check.c:1061-1079 feeds five non-snapshot strings, all refused at the header. test/dvs_check.c only hibernates and restores well-formed snapshots. bindings/ contain no restore-tampering tests. So no malformed frame array is presented to `diluvium_shim_checkframes` anywhere in the tree, and removing any of its refusal guards cannot turn anything red — that conclusion needs no build to establish.

WHERE I DO NOT BACK THE REPORT: the doc-contradiction leg fails, and the headline overstates. See the correction. But the load-bearing part stands: script/fuzz_snapshot.py:98 states something about its own coverage that is false in this tree, and the layer §10.10 calls "required in M6" has no mitigation-removal evidence, which is exactly convention 2's failure mode and the same shape as §17's own "prototype-tampering cases were silently exercising the reference path instead of the inline path".

Confidence is "likely" rather than "certain" only because I could not run the suites, so the specific tallies (429/398/11/20, 2 crashes with the digest off) are unverified; none of them is load-bearing for the conclusion.

*Correction to the report:* Two legs of the report should be dropped before it is acted on.

1. NO DOC SENTENCE IS CONTRADICTED. I read the whole surrounding sections. doc/Messaging.md:1437 ("A structure-aware fuzzer is an acceptance criterion, not follow-up") is satisfied — the fuzzer exists and runs in `make all` (Makefile:461). doc/Messaging.md:2366-2368 ("Structure-aware mutation of a snapshot; assert refusal, never a crash... 430 mutants, 0 crashes") is true as written; refusal by digest is still refusal, and §10.10's stated claim is "a malformed snapshot is refused" (doc:1460), which the run does support. doc/Messaging.md:2534-2536 and 2129-2132 present the numbers explicitly as a layer-by-layer experiment ("4 crashes with neither layer, 3 with the field checks, 0 with both"), which is the honest form, and 2131-2132 adds "the field checks are what stands between a deliberately rewritten snapshot and the interpreter" — i.e. the doc itself puts the field layer's threat model outside the fuzzer's corruption regime. src/dsnap.h:136-138 says it outright: "The fuzzer measures the first and the field checks are what its findings were fixed with." The reporter's "DOC CONTRADICTED, verbatim" heading is wrong; the doc's own text is where I confirmed the layering.

2. "0 of 429 reach diluvium_shim_checkframes" IS INACCURATE, and so is "zero effective coverage". An accepted mutant necessarily passed the digest and therefore did reach the validator (with unmutated fields); the reporter's own control — always returning DILUVIUM_RES_STACK aborts the run at fuzz_snapshot.py:177-180 — shows the accept path is covered and that a blanket-refuse regression would be caught. The defensible claim is narrower: no *malformed* frame array reaches `diluvium_shim_checkframes` anywhere in the tree, so none of its refusal guards has mitigation-removal evidence, and a guard that stops refusing ships silently.

3. THE FAILURE SCENARIO IS PROSPECTIVE, not a present wrong result. No input today makes this code produce a wrong answer; the field layer is correct and, per the digest-off measurement, load-bearing. What is broken is one false statement about coverage (script/fuzz_snapshot.py:98) and the evidence chain behind a layer §10.10 calls required in M6.

So the finding is worth fixing at the priority of "an acceptance criterion no longer exercises the layer it was named for", not "a security check is broken". The proposed fix is right in shape; note that a digest-recomputing pass must keep the existing digest-intact pass, and that the self-check should assert a non-zero count of field-layer refusals specifically, since a mutant refused by the digest and one refused by a frame guard are indistinguishable from the exit status alone.

<a id="f6"></a>

### 6. An endpoint reference can still be forged by any guest: the "hidden" metatable is reachable through debug.getregistry(), so a program mints a capability to any pre-authorised peer

`src/dendpoint.c`:150 &middot; blocks-building-on-it &middot; false-doc-claim &middot; audit `wrong-reason`

**Status: fixed.** Profile B's one item, and the last forgery route. The `debug` library is narrowed for instances: twelve of its sixteen functions are refusals that say why, `getregistry` and `getmetatable` among them. `the_registry_forgery_route_is_closed` and `the_debug_library_is_narrowed` (`dv_check`). A host that wants the whole library back asks by name with `DV_FLAG_UNSAFE_DEBUG`, which is profile A's configuration and puts the route back with it. Closing this also closed a budget escape the audit did not find: `debug.sethook()` took the one hook slot 9.4's budget lives in, so a program could switch its own budget off in one line (`a_guest_cannot_switch_its_own_budget_off`).

**Evidence.**

doc/Messaging.md:1880 states verbatim: '**"A reference cannot be forged" was false when written, and is now true.**' and names the three legs of the claim at 1881-1883: "there is no constructor anywhere, its metatable is hidden, and a lookalike is refused by name". §7.3 (doc/Messaging.md:561) says `ref` is "never constructed by the guest". The leg "its metatable is hidden" is false.

A reference is an ordinary Lua table wearing a private metatable (src/dendpoint.c:140-146 `de_push_ref`), and identity is tested by comparing that metatable (src/dendpoint.c:150-164 `de_ref_bytes`). `__metatable = false` (dendpoint.c:132-133) hides it from `getmetatable`, but the metatable itself is stored in the registry under `&DE_REF_MT` (dendpoint.c:135), and `dv_new` calls `luaL_openlibs` (src/dv.c:251), so `debug.getregistry()` is guest-callable. The metatable is found by its own `__name`, "diluvium.endpoint.ref" (dendpoint.c:129). No `debug.setmetatable` is needed -- plain `setmetatable` suffices, because the forged table starts with no metatable.

END-TO-END EXPLOIT, the same setup as the fixed msgpack.decode vector in a_guest_cannot_mint_a_reference (test/dv_check.c:714-748): host calls dv_endpoint_allow(inst, "9", 1, 42) and delivers NOTHING. Guest program:
  pcall(endpoint.bind, setmetatable({}, {}), 'prime')   -- makes de_ref_bytes call de_ref_mt, registering the mt
  for _, v in pairs(debug.getregistry()) do
    if type(v) == 'table' and rawget(v, '__name') == 'diluvium.endpoint.ref' then mt = v end end
  local forged = setmetatable({'9'}, mt)
  local okb, id = pcall(endpoint.bind, forged, 'peer')
  if okb then queue.push(id, 'STOLEN') end
Actual output of test/forge_check.c (built against dv.h alone, in this worktree):
  === can a guest forge an endpoint reference? ===
        guest said: mt_found=true
        guest said: bind=true|4
  [FAIL] no queue exists for the peer the guest tried to reach
        (token 42 is bound to queue handle 4)
        *** the host can read the guest's message to that peer: STOLEN
  1 checks, 1 failed
The decisive assertion is the one dv_check.c:745 already uses -- `dv_endpoint_queue(inst, 42) == 0` -- and it fails: the guest obtained a real endpoint handle to a peer it was never given, and its message arrived.

A bare-runtime probe shows the same forged object is accepted as a genuine reference (it gets past de_ref_bytes and fails only for want of a host handler): `endpoint.bind(forged) -> false  endpoint.bind: this host binds no endpoints, so there is nothing for a reference to resolve against` -- not "is not an endpoint reference".

Blast radius is bounded but is exactly the documented threat: the resolver's encode hook is NULL (src/dendpoint.c:182-184), so a guest cannot re-serialise a forged reference onward; reachable peers are those the host pre-authorised via dv_endpoint_allow, or any a dv_set_endpoint_handler callback would accept by name. That is the same reach the closed msgpack.decode vector had, and doc/Messaging.md:1888-1890 calls it "A reference was a guessable name, not a capability -- and 9.3's attenuation partly rests on it being a capability."

**Failure scenario.**

A host authorises one peer with dv_endpoint_allow(inst, ref_bytes, token) and hands the guest nothing. The guest reads the reference metatable out of debug.getregistry(), wraps guessed peer bytes in a table carrying it, calls endpoint.bind, and receives a live endpoint handle; every push on that handle is delivered to the authorised peer. Because 9.3's attenuation treats a reference as a capability, a child spawned with no endpoint of its own reaches any peer whose name it can guess, and the swarm's privilege hierarchy stops being structural. Verified end to end above.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Stop deriving reference identity from something a guest can construct. Either (a) make a reference a full userdata -- `lua_newuserdatauv` is not exposed to Lua, so a guest cannot mint one whatever metatable it can reach, and de_ref_bytes becomes a luaL_testudata-style check; or (b) keep a private registry-anchored weak-keyed set of the reference objects de_resolve actually created and have de_ref_bytes require membership, which is representation-independent. Do not rely on `__metatable` or on the registry key being unguessable: `debug.getregistry()` is iterable and the metatable self-identifies via `__name`. Then re-run test/forge_check.c and add it (and the debug.getmetatable-on-a-real-reference variant) to the forgery list in test_endpoint.lua alongside the fixext/ext8 vectors.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I tried to refute this and could not. Every quote is accurate and at the claimed line, and every link in the exploit chain holds against this tree.

QUOTES CONFIRMED VERBATIM AND AT THE CLAIMED LINES:
- src/dendpoint.c:150-164 `de_ref_bytes` — identity is nothing but metatable equality: `if (!lua_istable(L, idx)) return NULL; if (!lua_getmetatable(L, idx)) return NULL; de_ref_mt(L); if (lua_rawequal(L, -1, -2)) { lua_rawgeti(L, idx, 1); s = lua_tolstring(L, -1, len); ... }`. No provenance, no membership set, no userdata tag.
- src/dendpoint.c:129-135 — the metatable self-identifies (`lua_pushliteral(L, "diluvium.endpoint.ref"); lua_setfield(L, -2, "__name");`) and is anchored as a plain registry value (`lua_pushvalue(L, -1); lua_rawsetp(L, LUA_REGISTRYINDEX, &DE_REF_MT);`). `__metatable = false` at :132-133 hides it from `getmetatable` only.
- src/dendpoint.c:140-146 `de_push_ref` — a reference is an ordinary table plus that metatable.
- doc/Messaging.md:1880 verbatim: `**"A reference cannot be forged" was false when written, and is now true.**` with the three legs at :1881-1883.

REACHABILITY, TRACED MYSELF (not taken from the reporter):
- src/dv.c:251 `luaL_openlibs(inst->L);`; src/lualib.h:62 `#define luaL_openlibs(L) luaL_openselectedlibs(L, ~0, 0)`; src/linit.c:32 `{LUA_DBLIBNAME, luaopen_debug},`; src/ldblib.c:42 `db_getregistry` pushes `LUA_REGISTRYINDEX` and :458 registers it. src/dv.c:281-304 `dv_load` uses `luaL_loadbufferx` with no substituted `_ENV`, so the guest chunk has the real globals table. `endpoint` is a global via src/dlibs.c:22 + :34 `luaL_requiref(..., 1)`. Nothing in src/ nils out `debug`.
- Priming is guest-doable and the tree proves it: `de_bind` calls `de_ref_bytes(L, 1, &len)` as its very first statement, before `luaL_checkstring(L, 2)`, so any table carrying any metatable forces `de_ref_mt` to create and register the entry. test/test_endpoint.lua:63 already passes exactly such a value (`setmetatable({}, {})`) through `endpoint.bind`.
- Forging is not blocked by the protected-metatable rule: src/lbaselib.c:139-148 `luaB_setmetatable` errors only when *arg 1's existing* metatable has `__metatable`; a fresh `{}` has none, and the function never inspects arg 2 for it. No `debug.setmetatable` needed, as claimed.
- The consequence is a real handle: with bytes matching a `diluvium_endpoint_allow` entry, `de_allowed` returns the token, the handler branch is skipped entirely, and `diluvium_queue_declare(L, name, 0, 0, 0, 1)` mints an endpoint queue mapped to that token — so `dv_endpoint_queue(inst, 42)` becomes non-zero, which is the exact assertion test/dv_check.c:745-746 uses as "the decisive check". I also checked the "host can read it" half: the queue is created with `exported = 0` (dqueue.c:969), but `diluvium_queue_peek_bytes` (src/dqueue.c:1065-1088) has no export gate, so `dv_queue_pop` on that handle does return the guest's payload.

REFUTATION ATTEMPTS THAT FAILED:
- Doc context: I read §7.3 whole (doc/Messaging.md:553-575) and the §17 item (doc/Messaging.md:2638-2650). Neither qualifies the claim — no "assuming the host strips the debug library", no mention of `debug.getregistry` anywhere in the document (the only `debug` hits are `getinfo`/build-config, lines 719-2598). The claim at :1880 is unconditional, and §17 calls the closed vector "the most serious correction in the document", so this is the same class of failure re-opened by a different route.
- Test coverage: I grepped test/, script/ and bindings/ for the behaviour, not the name. `getregistry` appears only in upstream test/api.lua:29,1410 and test/db.lua:328,871. The forgery list at test/test_endpoint.lua:63-64 contains only value shapes that lack the metatable plus the two closed msgpack vectors — by construction none of them can reach the `lua_rawequal` true branch, so the loop cannot detect this vector. This is convention 2's failure mode again: the block titled "a reference cannot be forged" passes green while never exercising the one branch that matters.

CAVEAT ON THE REPORTER'S EVIDENCE (does not change the verdict): the cited artifact `test/forge_check.c` does not exist in this checkout (test/ contains dhash_check.c, dshim_check.c, dsnap_check.c, dtask_check.c, dv_check.c, dvs_check.c and no forge_check.c), so its pasted output is unverifiable here. My verdict rests only on static reading of the files and lines named above; I did not compile or run anything, per the audit constraint.

*Correction to the report:* Two corrections to the report, neither of which weakens it.

1. The proposed fix (a) — "make a reference a full userdata" — conflicts with M6 and would trade a security hole for a hibernation regression. src/dsnap.c:1045-1048 refuses every full userdata: `return luaL_error(L, "snapshot: this userdata cannot be captured -- persisted userdata (ext 0x05, the '__persist' hook) ... release its userdata first (10.7 item 2)");` and doc/Messaging.md:1931 records M6 as "done, except userdata `__persist`, which is refused by name", with doc/Messaging.md:2135 "Not done: userdata `__persist` (ext 0x05)." A reference is today a table and round-trips a snapshot; as a userdata, any instance merely *holding* an unbound reference — the normal state for a coordinator that received a peer's address and has not bound it yet — becomes unsnapshottable. Fix (b), the registry-anchored weak-keyed set of objects `de_resolve` actually created, is the one compatible with M6, though note it needs a deliberate decision about what happens to that set across `dv_snapshot`/`dv_restore`: a restored reference that is not in the rebuilt set would stop binding, so the set must be repopulated on the trusted restore path (which already reaches the resolver, per doc/Messaging.md:1893-1896) or references become single-lifetime objects.

2. The title's framing "the 'hidden' metatable is reachable" understates which doc leg breaks. `__metatable = false` does make it hidden from `getmetatable`, so that leg is literally true as written; what is false is the load-bearing conclusion at doc/Messaging.md:1880 — that hiding it from `getmetatable` plus having no constructor makes forgery structurally impossible. The accurate statement is that reference identity is derived from a value the guest can obtain and attach, so hiding the metatable from one accessor was never the property the claim needed.

<a id="f7"></a>

### 7. A spawn request's budget written the way 9.1 shows it is silently ignored, so the child runs unbudgeted

`/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c`:892 &middot; blocks-building-on-it &middot; correctness &middot; audit `new-c`

**Status: fixed.** Same defect as #2, found independently by a second audit.

**Evidence.**

doc/Messaging.md:813, inside 9.1's spawn example, is verbatim:

      budget = { instructions = 5e6, memory_kb = 512 },

do_spawn reads the two numbers at the *top level* of the request instead:

  src/dvs.c:892   field_int(msg, len, "instructions", &insns);
  src/dvs.c:893   field_int(msg, len, "memory_kb", &mem);

and diluvium_mp_field (src/dmsgpack.c:2101) walks only the keys of one map, skipping each value; it never descends. So the keys inside `budget` are never seen. build() then skips dv_set_budget entirely (src/dvs.c:362: `if (req->instructions != 0 || req->memory_kb != 0)`).

The adjacent comment at src/dvs.c:794 makes the miss explicit: "A budget written as 5e6 arrives as a float, which is what 9.1's own example shows -- so accepting one is reading the document rather than being lax." The example was read for the number format and not for the nesting.

Measured. /home/user/diluvium/test/audit_budget.c spawns the same child (a 3,000,000-iteration loop) twice, once with 9.1's shape and once with top-level keys:

  gcc -O1 -g -DLUA_USE_LINUX -Wl,-E -DMAKE_LIB -I.data -o dist/audit_budget \
      test/audit_budget.c .data/dvs.c .data/onelua.c -lm -ldl
  ./dist/audit_budget
  -- budget written as 9.1 shows it: budget = {instructions=2e5, ...} --
     dvs_budget(child) -> instructions=0 memory_kb=0
     step 1: spawned:2
  -- budget written as do_spawn reads it: instructions=2e5 at the top level --
     step 1: spawned:2
     step 2: exceeded:2

The doc's shape yields no budget at all and the loop runs to completion; the flat shape is stopped with `exceeded`.

**Failure scenario.**

A supervisor copies 9.1's spawn request and pushes {op='spawn', code=C, caps={}, budget={instructions=2e5, memory_kb=512}}. dvs_budget reports instructions=0 memory_kb=0, dv_set_budget is never called, no count hook is armed and no memory ceiling is set. The child then runs a loop of any length to completion and no `exceeded` event is ever emitted. 9.4 says "A guest cannot meaningfully limit itself, because a runaway loop never yields and nothing cooperative will stop it", and the M7 record at doc/Messaging.md:2149 lists "budgets" as **All met.** — so the one mechanism that stops a runaway is absent, silently, for a program written to the document.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Read the budget from either shape: try the nested `budget` map first (open a cursor, diluvium_mp_field(&c,"budget"), then read "instructions"/"memory_kb" from that sub-map) and fall back to the top-level keys, or correct 9.1's example to the flat shape and record it in section 17. Either way add a dvs_check case that asserts dvs_budget() returns what the request asked for, since nothing currently reads the budget back after a spawn.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I tried to refute this and could not. Every link verified against this tree:

1. QUOTES ARE ACCURATE AT THE CLAIMED LINES. doc/Messaging.md:813 is verbatim `  budget = { instructions = 5e6, memory_kb = 512 },` inside 9.1's `queue.push(sys, {...})` example (lines 807-816). src/dvs.c:892-893 are verbatim `field_int(msg, len, "instructions", &insns);` / `field_int(msg, len, "memory_kb", &mem);`.

2. THE READER GENUINELY DOES NOT DESCEND. src/dmsgpack.c:2101-2118: `diluvium_mp_field` reads one token, requires `tok.kind == DILUVIUM_MP_MAP`, then loops `for (n = tok.len; n > 0; n--)` comparing each key and calling `diluvium_mp_skip(c)` on every non-matching value. `field_int` (src/dvs.c:788-802) calls `diluvium_mp_open` on the whole message, so it searches only top-level keys. `instructions` is not one — the top level is op/code/caps/budget/wake_on_message — so `field_int` returns 0 and the `uint64_t insns = 0, mem = 0;` initializers at src/dvs.c:787 survive.

3. THE SKIP IS SILENT, NOT A FALLBACK. src/dvs.c:362 is verbatim `if (req->instructions != 0 || req->memory_kb != 0) {` guarding the only `dv_set_budget` call in build() (line 363). Both zero, so no budget, no count hook, no memory ceiling. I grepped every `budget` occurrence in src/dvs.c (lines 53,54,64,256,263,349,359-364,682-686,794) — there is no nested-map path anywhere and no second read of the field.

4. REACHABLE, WITH A TRACED CALLER. src/dvs.c:1085-1090: drain reads `op` from the same top-level map with the same `field_str`, and `if (strcmp(op, "spawn") == 0) do_spawn(sw, sl, (const char *)buf, n);`. `buf` is the raw encoding of the guest's pushed table — proven by the fact that `op` and `code` are found at top level in the working spawn tests (test/dvs_check.c:243, 360, 733). So the flat keys the code wants and the nested keys the doc writes are read from the same map.

5. NO LOUD FAILURE SAVES IT. I checked whether the encoder might reject a nested table, which would make the mismatch noisy rather than silent. It does not: src/dmsgpack.c:767-788 encodes nested tables to a depth cap (`MP_MAX_DEPTH`), so `budget = {...}` encodes cleanly as a nested map and is then skipped unread.

6. THE PROPERTY IS UNTESTED — I grepped rather than trusting the report. `instructions|memory_kb|budget` across test/dvs_check.c yields only the `dvs_root` test at lines 830-891, which passes the budget as flat C scalars: `dvs_root(sw, src, strlen(src), caps, 3, 5000000, 4096, &root)` (line 840) and asserts `insns == 5000000` (847). Not one of the eleven spawn-message literals in dvs_check.c (144, 243, 360, 422, 466, 470, 519, 550, 733, 923) carries a budget in any shape. test/mp_cursor_fuzz.c:163-164 lists `"instructions"` only as a fuzz key for a cursor-stays-in-bounds walk (lines 169-180), not a semantic check. Nothing in test/, script/ or bindings/ pushes a budget through the message path.

7. THE DOC DOES NOT STATE THE LIMITATION LATER — I checked the whole area, per instruction. `grep -n "memory_kb\|instructions"` over the entire doc returns line 813 and two unrelated hits (1251 about debug-vs-release opcode counts, 2249 about wasm exception opcodes). Line 813 is therefore the doc's ONLY statement of the spawn-request budget shape. 9.2-9.5 each carry explicit build-time corrections where the draft was wrong (9.3's "**As built:**" at 910, 9.5's "**Two corrections from building it.**" at 941) and none mentions the budget shape. Section 17 has no entry for it.

8. THE CODE ITSELF TREATS 9.1's EXAMPLE AS NORMATIVE, which kills the "it's only illustrative pseudocode" defence. src/dvs.c:794-795: "A budget written as 5e6 arrives as a float, which is what 9.1's own example shows -- so accepting one is reading the document rather than being lax." The author cited that exact line as the spec, took the float format from it, and missed the nesting.

Corroborating: M7's own mutation list at doc:2169-2172 names "attenuation, the lifecycle gate, the spawn-rate deferral, subtree recursion, the descendant rule on kill, handle non-reuse, the table bound, the wake buffer's bound, `wake_on_message`, and the placement of the buffer drain" — budgets are absent, consistent with there being no test to remove a mitigation from. Yet doc:2148-2149 says "**M7: swarm layer** — done." and lists "budgets" among what was delivered.

Failure scenario is concrete and follows from 1-5: a supervisor that copies 9.1 verbatim gets a child with no instruction budget and no memory ceiling, and 9.4 at doc:918-919 says "A guest cannot meaningfully limit itself, because a runaway loop never yields and nothing cooperative will stop it" — so the runaway the budget exists to stop runs to completion and no `exceeded` event is ever emitted.

*Correction to the report:* The defect is real and the mechanism is exactly as described, but two evidence claims in the report should not be repeated as-is:

(a) The "Measured" section cites /home/user/diluvium/test/audit_budget.c and a gcc invocation. That file does not exist in this checkout (`ls` returns "No such file or directory"; it is absent from test/ and from the .data/ amalgamation dir). The reported dvs_budget(child) -> instructions=0 memory_kb=0 / spawned-vs-exceeded transcript is therefore not reproducible from the tree as it stands. The finding does not need it — the static path (dmsgpack.c:2101-2118 skips the nested map, so dvs.c:892-893 read nothing, so dvs.c:362 skips dv_set_budget) is airtight on its own — but the audit should rest on the static proof, not on a transcript from a deleted file. Note also that producing it required writing and compiling in a shared checkout.

(b) "nothing currently reads the budget back after a spawn" is too broad. test/dvs_check.c:846 does read it back (`ok(dvs_budget(sw, root, &insns, &mem) == DVS_OK, "the budget reads back");`), asserting insns == 5000000 and mem == 4096 — but only for an instance created by dvs_root with flat C scalar arguments (line 840). The accurate statement is narrower and still damning: no test reads the budget back after a *message-driven* spawn, which is why the parser mismatch has gone unnoticed.

One nuance on the M7 quote: the "**All met.**" at doc:2153 attaches to the three accept clauses at 2151-2153 (spawn/restart, attenuation, wake-and-order), none of which is about budgets. The doc's claim that budgets are done rests on the delivery list at 2149 under "**M7: swarm layer** — done.", not on "All met." The primary contradiction to cite is doc:813 against src/dvs.c:892.
