<a id="f18"></a>

### 18. dsnap_check's "every header refusal code has its own sentence" checks neither distinctness nor the last two codes, so DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT can lose its sentence with the test green

`test/dsnap_check.c`:1058 &middot; should-fix &middot; unchecked-property &middot; audit `wrong-reason`

**Status: fixed.** Both halves. `DILUVIUM_SNAP_LAST` is what the loop walks, the sentences are compared pairwise, and a third assertion checks the marker itself so adding a code and forgetting to move it is caught. Both mutations this finding recorded as green are red.

**Evidence.**

test/dsnap_check.c:1055-1064:
  static void every_refusal_has_its_own_sentence (lua_State *L) {
    int c, distinct = 1;
    for (c = 0; c <= DILUVIUM_SNAP_HOST_MISMATCH; c++) {
      const char *r = diluvium_snap_why(c);
      if (r == NULL || *r == '\0' ||
          strcmp(r, diluvium_snap_why(DILUVIUM_SNAP_HOST_MISMATCH + 50)) == 0)
        distinct = 0;
    }
    ok(distinct, "every header refusal code has its own sentence");
Two defects. The loop stops at DILUVIUM_SNAP_HOST_MISMATCH = 6 (src/dsnap.h:110) but the enum runs to DILUVIUM_SNAP_BAD_PAYLOAD = 7 and DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT = 8 (dsnap.h:111-112), so the two newest codes -- the ones added by the §10.10 two-layer correction -- are never inspected. And it compares each sentence only against the "unknown reason" sentinel, never pairwise, so duplicates pass.

MUTATION A (loop bound): delete the `case DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT:` arm from diluvium_snap_why (src/dsnap.c:554-556) so code 8 falls through to `default: return "unknown reason";`
  make dsnap_check -> [PASS] every header refusal code has its own sentence / 142 checks, 0 failed

MUTATION B (distinctness): make DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT return BAD_PAYLOAD's sentence verbatim ("the header matched but the payload after it could not be read")
  make dsnap_check -> [PASS] every header refusal code has its own sentence / 142 checks, 0 failed

Both mutations leave the test green. The sentence is host-visible: src/dsnap.c:1786 does `if (out_msg != NULL) *out_msg = diluvium_snap_why(rc);`, and DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT is what the payload digest produces (src/dsnap.c:520-521).

The sibling test has the same distinctness gap but a correct bound: test/dshim_check.c:923-934 `reasons_are_all_worded` loops `c <= DILUVIUM_SNAP_C_FRAME`, which is the largest code (3), so only pairwise comparison is missing there.

**Failure scenario.**

Someone refactors diluvium_snap_why -- merges the BAD_PAYLOAD and CORRUPT arms, or drops the CORRUPT arm while renumbering -- and a host restoring a truncated or altered snapshot is told "unknown reason" or is told the payload "could not be read" when in fact the digest mismatched. Those are different operator actions (re-fetch the bytes vs. treat the snapshot as tampered), and dsnap.h:123 distinguishes them deliberately: "This is *integrity*, not authentication". The one test that exists to prevent it reports PASS in both cases, as shown above.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Bound the loop by the real maximum (`c <= DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT`) and compare pairwise rather than against the sentinel only: collect the strings and assert every pair differs, keeping the sentinel check for the non-empty/non-unknown property. Same change to test/dshim_check.c:926 for pairwise comparison. Better still, drive the loop from a single list of codes shared with dsnap.h so a code added later is covered without editing the bound -- the failure here is exactly the "naming must not depend on history" shape §17 already records.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=likely.**

Every quote is accurate and at the stated line, and I could not construct a refutation.

CODE VERIFIED
- test/dsnap_check.c:1055-1064 matches the reported text verbatim; the bound `for (c = 0; c <= DILUVIUM_SNAP_HOST_MISMATCH; c++)` is at 1058, the only comparison `strcmp(r, diluvium_snap_why(DILUVIUM_SNAP_HOST_MISMATCH + 50))` at 1061 (56 -> `default: return "unknown reason"`), and the assertion `ok(distinct, "every header refusal code has its own sentence")` at 1064. The test is registered and runs: test/dsnap_check.c:2305.
- src/dsnap.h:110-112 confirms HOST_MISMATCH 6, BAD_PAYLOAD 7, CORRUPT 8. So the loop inspects 7 of the 9 codes, and never compares any two sentences to each other. Both reported defects are real.

REACHABILITY / SCOPE OBJECTION FAILS
The strongest defence would be that codes 7 and 8 are payload refusals, not "header refusal codes", making the bound correct for the property named. That fails for code 8: `rc = DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT;` at src/dsnap.c:521 sits inside `diluvium_snap_checkheader` (the digest carried in the header), so checkheader itself returns 8. The header's own contract (src/dsnap.h:97) directs callers to `diluvium_snap_why` for exactly these codes.

COVERAGE SEARCHED BY BEHAVIOUR, NOT NAME
The only `diluvium_snap_why` call sites in the tree are src/dsnap.c:1786 and test/dsnap_check.c:790, 878, 1059, 1061 (dsnap.h:97/141/224 are prose/decl). 790 and 878 pass a runtime `rc`, and no test in the tree ever produces rc==8: the sole header-plus-payload case, test/dsnap_check.c:796-807, appends "hi" to a header written with no digest, which the `if (*want != '\0')` guard at src/dsnap.c:515 skips. grep for SNAP_CORRUPT / SNAP_BAD_PAYLOAD across test/, script/ and bindings/ returns nothing. grep -i for corrupt|tamper|truncat|digest over the C tests and fuzzers turns up only prototype-hash tampering (dsnap_check.c:1547,1590) and script/fuzz_snapshot.py, which measures refused-vs-crashed, not sentence identity. test/dv_check.c:1063-1080 and test/snap_harness.c assert only "not DV_OK". Nothing anywhere compares two refusal sentences pairwise. So both mutations are invisible to the suite by construction: deleting the `case DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT:` arm (src/dsnap.c:554-556) or making it return BAD_PAYLOAD's string cannot be observed by any assertion in the tree. I could not run make (prohibited), but no observing code path exists, so the reported green results follow deterministically.

CONSEQUENCE IS HOST-VISIBLE
src/dsnap.c:1786 `if (out_msg != NULL) *out_msg = diluvium_snap_why(rc);` flows to src/dv.c:756, which stores that sentence via set_error and returns DV_SNAPSHOT_MISMATCH. dsnap.h:123-126 deliberately separates "corrupted in transit" from "could not be read", so collapsing them misdirects the operator.

RESERVATION (why "likely", not "certain")
The wrong output is mutation-conditional: today all nine sentences are present and distinct, so nothing is currently mis-reported. Admissibility rests on convention 2 ("a green test is evidence only after its mitigation has been removed once and the test observed to fail") rather than on a live wrong result, and doc/Messaging.md makes no claim about sentence distinctness, so there is no doc contradiction to lean on. Within convention 2 this is squarely a wrong-reason test: line 1064 prints a claim about every refusal code that the loop at 1058 does not evaluate for two of them and does not test at all for distinctness.

*Correction to the report:* Two narrowings, neither of which touches the headline.

1. DILUVIUM_SNAP_BAD_PAYLOAD (7) is NOT returned by diluvium_snap_checkheader; its only source is diluvium_snap_load's payload pcall at src/dsnap.c:1805. So describing 7 as a missed "header refusal code" is arguable on the test's own wording. The load-bearing half is DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT (8), which checkheader does return (src/dsnap.c:521) and which the loop genuinely misses. The finding's title and failure scenario already centre on CORRUPT, so it survives.

2. The "142 checks, 0 failed" run output could not be reproduced (building is prohibited in this pass), and doc/Messaging.md line 2055 states "`dsnap_check.c` is 140 checks". The count is immaterial; the mutation survival is established from source coverage, not from the reported totals.

Also worth attaching to the fix: no test asserts that a digest mismatch yields DILUVIUM_SNAP_CORRUPT at all (grep for the code across test/ is empty), so the code-8 path has neither a behavioural nor a wording assertion. Extending the loop bound and adding a pairwise compare fixes the wording half; a digest-mismatch case through diluvium_snap_checkheader would be needed for the other.

<a id="f19"></a>

### 19. A refused hibernate makes a child's clean exit report as 'faulted' to its parent

`/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c`:1174 &middot; should-fix &middot; correctness &middot; audit `new-c`

**Status: fixed.** The same sticky-error fix as #15, and this is the route `a_denied_hibernate_does_not_make_a_clean_exit_a_fault` (`dvs_check`) reproduces, because it is the most likely way to reach the symptom from a program. Note it needs `dvs_allow_hibernation`: since build3 the default refuses before `dv_snapshot` is reached, so nothing is left on the child.

**Evidence.**

src/dvs.c:1174 decides which of three events to send by asking whether the instance holds an error string:

      int faulted = (dv_last_error(sl->inst) != NULL);

dv_last_error is a sticky buffer that dv.c also writes for refusals that have nothing to do with the program's own execution. dvs_hibernate reaches one of them at src/dvs.c:631: dv_snapshot on an instance that has not run sets "dv_snapshot: nothing is parked..." on that instance (src/dv.c:697-701) and never clears it. The comment three lines below the bug (src/dvs.c:1176-1181) states the invariant it breaks: "Three reasons an instance stops, and they are reported as three different events because a supervisor's response to each is different: a clean exit may need nothing, a fault may need a restart..."

Measured. /home/user/diluvium/test/audit_probe.c has a supervisor push {op='spawn'} and {op='hibernate', id=2} in one batch, so both are drained before the child is first driven; the child is `local q = queue.declare('done', {cap = 2})`, which exits cleanly.

  gcc -DLUA_USER_H='"ltests.h"' -O0 -g -DLUA_USE_LINUX -Wl,-E -DMAKE_LIB -I.data \
      -o dist/audit_probe test/audit_probe.c .data/dvs.c .data/onelua.c -lm -ldl
  ./dist/audit_probe
  -- probe 1: what the supervisor was told --
     step 1: spawned|2|nil
     step 1: denied|2|instance 2 will not hibernate: dv_snapshot: nothing is parked; ...
     step 2: faulted|2|dv_snapshot: nothing is parked; a snapshot is taken of a program waiting on a queue, not of one that has not run or has finished
  -- control: the same child, no hibernate request --
     step 1: spawned|2|nil
     step 2: exited|2|nil

The control shows the identical child reported as `exited`. The only difference is the earlier, correctly-denied hibernate.

**Failure scenario.**

A supervisor spawns a worker and, in the same batch of lifecycle requests, asks for it to be hibernated (a natural 'start it asleep' pattern). The hibernate is correctly denied - nothing is parked yet. The worker then finishes its job and exits cleanly, and the supervisor receives event='faulted' with a snapshot error as the detail. The restart policy in test/dvs_check.c's own SUPERVISOR - "if e.event == 'exited' or e.event == 'faulted' ... start()" - would restart it, and a supervisor that restarts only on faults now restarts a worker that succeeded, indefinitely if it repeats the pattern.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Do not use the presence of an error string as a proxy for 'the last run raised'. Either clear it in dv.c at the top of dv_run/dv_resume (set_error(inst, NULL)) so dv_last_error always describes the most recent execution, or record dv_last_error(sl->inst) before calling host.drive and treat only a *changed* value as a fault. Add a dvs_check case for 'a child that exits cleanly after a denied hibernate is reported as exited', which currently fails.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I confirmed the finding from source and could not refute it.

CITATIONS VERIFIED VERBATIM AND AT THE CLAIMED LINES.
- src/dvs.c:1174 is exactly `      int faulted = (dv_last_error(sl->inst) != NULL);`, and the invariant comment the reporter quotes ("Three reasons an instance stops...") is at src/dvs.c:1175-1181.
- src/dvs.c:631 is `if (dv_snapshot(sl->inst, NULL, NULL, 0, &need) != DV_OK) {`, whose branch (632-634) reads `dv_last_error(sl->inst)` — proving dv_snapshot has already written the instance's buffer.
- src/dv.c:698-701 is the guard `if (!inst->started || inst->finished) { set_error(inst, "dv_snapshot: nothing is parked; a snapshot is taken of a program waiting on a queue, not of one that has not run or has finished");` (the reporter's "697-701" is off by the `L = inst->L;` line only).

STICKINESS IS REAL. The only writers of inst->error are set_error/set_error_from (src/dv.c:89-105). dv_run (src/dv.c:545-582) does not clear it on entry, and settle's success branch (src/dv.c:516-521) sets finished/parked and returns DV_DONE without clearing it. dv_free (src/dv.c:271-278) frees it, which is too late. So a snapshot refusal recorded before the program ever ran is still what dv_last_error returns after the program exits cleanly.

REACHABILITY TRACED TO A CALLER, TWICE OVER.
(1) The reporter's path: drain (src/dvs.c:1089-1096) dispatches `spawn` and then `hibernate` from the same lifecycle queue in one pass; claim (src/dvs.c:341, `sw->slots[i].id = sw->next_id++;` with next_id seeded to 1 at src/dvs.c:176) makes the root's first child deterministically id 2, so `{op='hibernate', id=2}` resolves; do_hibernate's descendant walk (src/dvs.c:975-982) passes; dvs_hibernate reaches dv_snapshot because sl->inst != NULL and !started, so the instance error is set and "denied" is emitted (src/dvs.c:998). dvs_step's drive loop (src/dvs.c:1163-1168) then drives that child in the SAME step — its own comment says "A spawn requested this step therefore starts running in the same step". With the reference host (host_drive, test/dvs_check.c:71-97) a program that runs to completion returns DV_DONE so keep == 0, dv_exceeded is 0 with no budget, and line 1174 classifies the clean exit as "faulted", with the snapshot message as the detail (src/dvs.c:1193-1203).
(2) A path needing no id-guessing and no same-batch trick: a supervisor hibernates an already-parked descendant whose state the snapshot layer legitimately refuses — src/dsnap.c:1015 "this C function is not in the permanents", :1045 "this userdata cannot be captured", :1410 "frame %d of this thread is a C function". dvs_hibernate returns DVS_ERROR, the child keeps running, finishes cleanly, and its parent is told "faulted".

REFUTATIONS I TRIED AND THAT FAILED.
- Does an earlier check prevent it? No: dvs_hibernate's early returns are `sl == NULL || !sl->alive` (DVS_GONE) and `sl->inst == NULL` (DVS_OK); a freshly spawned child hits neither.
- Does the failed hibernate kill or clear the slot? No, it only returns DVS_ERROR after set_error on the swarm.
- Is the precondition contrived? No — test/dvs_check.c:879-882 already asserts `ok(dvs_hibernate(sw, root) != DVS_OK, "an instance that has not run yet cannot be hibernated, since it is not parked");`, so this is a sanctioned, host-visible state. That test's instance is the root (parent 0), so no event is emitted and the misclassification is invisible to it — which is why the suite is green.
- Does the doc state the limitation later? I read all of §9.2 (doc/Messaging.md:872-899), §9.5 (930-965) and §10.1-10.2 (966-1010) plus every occurrence of "faulted"/"exited" in the doc (lines 876, 896, 853, 2151-2174). Nothing licenses collapsing a refusal into a fault. The opposite: line 896 "The events are `spawned`, `exited`, `faulted`, `exceeded`, ..." treats them as distinct, and §10.2's "Implementation requirement" ends "Return a clean error naming the situation", i.e. a refused hibernate is a designed, non-fatal outcome.
- Did I grep for existing coverage of the behaviour rather than the name? Yes — every hibernate/faulted/exited site in test/dvs_check.c (lines 249, 320, 336, 620-681, 730-778, 879-884, 899-961). The fault test (a_faulting_child_reports_a_readable_reason, test/dvs_check.c:915-968) only ever asserts a fault IS reported; no test asserts that a clean exit is NOT reported as a fault after any refusal.

The reporter's restart-policy quote is also accurate: test/dvs_check.c:249 is `"  if e.event == 'exited' or e.event == 'faulted' or e.event == 'exceeded' then\n"` followed by `start()`.

*Correction to the report:* Three amendments, none of which weaken the finding.

1. EVIDENCE CAVEAT. /home/user/diluvium/test/audit_probe.c does not exist in this checkout (nor dist/audit_probe), so the pasted transcript cannot be re-checked here, and the audit's no-write constraint forbids recreating it. The finding does not need it: the static trace above is complete and every quoted line is confirmed. Whoever accepts this should treat the transcript as unverified and the source trace as the evidence.

2. A BETTER CITATION THAN THE REPORTER USED. The reporter leans on a code comment for the broken invariant. The header contract is more decisive: src/dv.h:116-118 says of dv_last_error, "The message from the last DV_ERROR on this instance, or NULL. Owned by the instance and valid until the next call on it." dv_snapshot's refusal returns DV_ERROR, so a refusal is exactly what that sentence says the buffer holds. src/dvs.c:1174 therefore misuses a documented "last DV_ERROR of any kind" as "the last execution raised". doc/Messaging.md:1574 repeats it: "`dv_last_error` returns the message and traceback for the last `DV_ERROR`".

3. A WIDER FAILURE SCENARIO THAN THE ONE REPORTED. The reported scenario needs the supervisor to guess the child's id in the same batch as the spawn (sound here — ids are sequential from 1, so the first child is always 2 — but it does look contrived). The mechanism does not need that. Any refused snapshot of a *parked* descendant poisons the same predicate: src/dsnap.c:1015 ("this C function is not in the permanents"), :1045 ("this userdata cannot be captured"), :1410 ("frame %d of this thread is a C function"). A supervisor that learns a child's id from its "spawned" event, asks to hibernate it while it is parked holding an uncapturable value, is correctly denied, and then sees the child finish its work, is told event='faulted'. That is the ordinary path and it should be the scenario recorded.

Also worth noting for whoever fixes it: the proposed fix "clear it at the top of dv_run/dv_resume" is not sufficient on its own for path (2) above, because there the refusal happens between a dv_resume and the final dv_resume that completes the program — clearing on entry to dv_resume does handle it, but clearing only in dv_run would not. The alternative proposal (snapshot dv_last_error before host.drive and treat only a changed value as a fault) is also fragile if the message is identical. The robust fix is for the host vtable's drive to report the dv_status it saw, or for dv.c to expose a "the last execution ended in an error" flag distinct from the sticky message buffer.

<a id="f20"></a>

### 20. kill_subtree recurses once per link, which its own comment says it does not; a deep chain segfaults dvs_kill

`/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c`:569 &middot; should-fix &middot; false-doc-claim &middot; audit `new-c`

**Status: fixed.** Mark and sweep over the flat table. **The crash this finding described is not reachable** -- see the note below -- and the fix is so the code matches its own comment.

**Evidence.**

src/dvs.c:568-573 says verbatim:

  ** Children first, and by repeated scan rather than a recursive walk: the table
  ** is flat, a subtree can be as deep as delegation went, and 9.1.1 says depth is
  ** unbounded. A recursive walk would meet the C stack at a depth a program is
  ** entitled to reach.

src/dvs.c:578 is `kill_subtree(sw, sw->slots[i].id, 0);` - a self-call. The repeated scan replaces only the sibling iteration (so freeing a slot cannot invalidate the scan); depth is still one C frame per level.

doc/Messaging.md:837 backs the hazard: "Parentage is a single field per instance, depth is unbounded, and subtree kill means what it says."

Measured. /home/user/diluvium/test/audit_chain.c builds a chain of self-replicating agents that each spawn a copy of themselves and then hibernate (so a link costs a ~2 KB snapshot rather than an interpreter), with a depth counter and stack-address probe compiled into kill_subtree under -DAUDIT_DEPTH:

  ./dist/audit_chain 2000
  chain built: 2000 instances alive, root=1
  dvs_kill(root): kill_subtree recursed 2000 deep, 127936 bytes of C stack (64 bytes per frame)
  alive after the kill: 0

One frame per link, 64 bytes each at -O1. And the crash:

  bash -c 'ulimit -s 256; ./dist/audit_chain 6000; echo "exit=$?"'
  chain built: 6000 instances alive, root=1
  bash: line 1:  14125 Segmentation fault      ./dist/audit_chain 6000
  exit=139

The chain builds fine; the fault is inside dvs_kill.

**Failure scenario.**

A host gives the swarm a large instance table (dvs_new's max_instances) and its agents delegate the lifecycle capability down a long chain, which 9.1.1 explicitly permits and 9.1.2 item 2 supports with a single parent field. dvs_kill on a high ancestor - or the same call reached from dvs_free, or from do_kill on a lifecycle request - recurses once per link at 64 bytes a frame. On a thread with a 256 KB stack a 6000-link chain segfaults, as measured above; on an 8 MB main-thread stack the crossover is around 131,000 links. The failure is a SIGSEGV with no provenance, in exactly the layer whose job is to clean up after a failing subtree.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Make the walk genuinely iterative: repeatedly scan for any live slot that has no live children and is a descendant of the target, releasing it, until the target itself goes - or keep an explicit worklist bounded by nslots (the table is already the bound). Either way rewrite the comment to describe what the code does. A regression test can assert the maximum recursion depth stays 1, since a stack-overflow test needs a big chain.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I tried to refute this and could not. Verified independently:

CODE QUOTE ACCURATE AT THE STATED LINES. src/dvs.c:568-573 is verbatim as quoted ("by repeated scan rather than a recursive walk ... A recursive walk would meet the C stack at a depth a program is entitled to reach"), and src/dvs.c:578 is `kill_subtree(sw, sw->slots[i].id, 0);` inside `static void kill_subtree` (opened line 561). The outer `for (;;)` restarts only the sibling scan; the descent is the self-call, so depth is one C frame per delegation level and the comment's material claim is false about its own code.

MACHINE-VERIFIED WITHOUT REBUILDING. The tree's committed dist/dvs.o disassembles with `<kill_subtree>` at 8f0 containing `98d: call 8f0 <kill_subtree>` followed by `992: mov 0x28(%r14),%edx` -- a real, non-tail call, so no tail-call optimization collapses it. Frame is 5 callee-saved pushes + return address.

REACHABLE. Four callers: dvs_kill (596), do_kill (945) on a guest system/lifecycle request, and dvs_step's fault/exit paths (1149, 1201) which need no host involvement.

DEPTH GENUINELY UNBOUNDED BY THIS LAYER. do_spawn sets sl->parent = parent->id (906); dvs_may_grant is `return dvs_holds(sw, parent, cap);` (299) so re-granting lifecycle unattenuated is permitted; no depth field or check exists in the file; dvs_new caps nslots at nothing but the host's uint32_t. doc/Messaging.md:2426 makes the deep chain a design invariant: "the lifecycle capability delegates recursively with no additional mechanism."

MEASUREMENT NOT FABRICATED (my first suspicion). test/audit_chain.c and dist/audit_chain are absent from the main checkout but present in /home/user/diluvium/.claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-9/; the binary carries audit_depth/audit_maxdepth/audit_lowsp/audit_highsp in BSS; the harness builds a true chain (one child per generation, then hibernate). Their src/dvs.c is now git-diff-clean, so the instrumentation was reverted after measuring -- but the recursion is provable from the unmodified source and object alone.

NO PRIOR DISCLOSURE. I read 9.1.1 whole, 9.1.2, 9.5's failure-policy table, the swarm milestone section (2150-2200), the invariant list (2415-2438) and section 17 (2439+): nothing names a stack-depth limit on subtree kill, so this is not an already-declared not-done item.

Two parts of the report are wrong; see correction. It still qualifies under clause (a): exact line plus a false statement, plus a demonstrated crash.

*Correction to the report:* Substance stands; recast the claim before recording it.

1. Kind is wrong: this is a false SOURCE COMMENT, not a false-doc-claim. doc/Messaging.md is not contradicted anywhere. doc:837 ("Parentage is a single field per instance, depth is unbounded, and subtree kill means what it says") states the hazard, it does not claim an iterative walk; and doc:2170 actually calls the mechanism "subtree recursion", which is CONSISTENT with the code. The false statement is src/dvs.c:568-573 only. Drop the doc quote from the claim and keep it as motivation.

2. Frame size 64 bytes was their instrumented build. The shipped dist/dvs.o pushes r14, r13, r12, rbp, rbx plus the return address = 48 bytes/frame, so the 8 MB main-thread crossover is ~175,000 links, not ~131,000. Since sizeof(dvs_slot) = 0x658 = 1624 bytes (the `imul $0x658` at 918), 175k links also costs ~284 MB of slot table before snapshots. The realistic trigger is therefore a host driving the swarm on a reduced-stack thread -- as demonstrated at 6000 links under `ulimit -s 256` -- not the default main thread.

3. dvs_free does NOT reach kill_subtree: it releases slots in a flat `for (i = 0; i < sw->nslots; i++)` loop. Strike that clause from the failure scenario. The three surviving paths are dvs_kill (596), do_kill (945), and dvs_step's fault/exit handling (1149, 1201) -- the last being the worst, since a mid-chain fault kills a deep subtree automatically with no host call.

<a id="f21"></a>

### 21. do_spawn truncates the spawned program at its first zero byte

`/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c`:909 &middot; should-fix &middot; correctness &middot; audit `new-c`

**Status: fixed.** `code_len` is carried instead of `strlen`. `a_spawn_is_not_truncated_at_a_zero_byte`.

**Evidence.**

field_str (src/dvs.c:772-783) copies the msgpack string's t.len bytes and NUL-terminates, so the buffer holds the full program including any zero bytes. do_spawn then throws the length away:

  src/dvs.c:909   req.code_len = strlen(code);

dvs_root, by contrast, is handed an explicit code_len (src/dvs.c:383-384, 403). The internal request field is documented at src/dvs.c:62 as "the program's source or bytecode", and dv_load uses mode "bt" (src/dv.c:290), so a binary chunk is a legal program.

Measured, /home/user/diluvium/test/audit_nul.c:

  gcc -O1 -g -DLUA_USE_LINUX -Wl,-E -DMAKE_LIB -I.data -o dist/audit_nul \
      test/audit_nul.c .data/dvs.c .data/onelua.c -lm -ldl
  ./dist/audit_nul
  -- a spawn whose source contains a zero byte --
     step 0: the guest asked to spawn 55 bytes
     step 1: spawned:2:nil
     step 2: exited:2:nil
     alive at the end: 1
  -- a spawn carrying precompiled bytecode --
     step 0: bytecode is 241 bytes, first zero at 33
     step 1: faulted:2:the program would not load: agent: bad binary format (truncated chunk)
     alive at the end: 1

In the first case only the 39 bytes before the zero were loaded: the child declared its queue and returned instead of parking on queue.wait, and the supervisor was told `spawned` then `exited` with no diagnostic at all. In the second, 241 bytes of string.dump output became 32 and the load failed.

**Failure scenario.**

A guest pushes {op='spawn', code=P} where P contains a zero byte - either a source string with an embedded \0 (a generated program with binary data in a literal, which 1.1's self-rewriting premise makes ordinary) or a precompiled chunk from string.dump. The swarm loads only the prefix. For source the child silently runs a different program and the parent receives a normal `spawned` event, so there is nothing to observe the difference by; for bytecode every spawn fails with "truncated chunk", making 10.5's content-addressed code path unreachable through system/lifecycle even though dvs_root handles the same bytes.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Have field_str report the length it copied (or add a field_bytes that does) and set req.code_len from it rather than from strlen. The 8192-byte `code` buffer and drain's `buf` already bound the size, so nothing else changes. Add a dvs_check case that spawns string.dump(load(src)) and asserts the child parks.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

Confirmed from source, not from the reporter's text. src/dvs.c:909 is exactly `req.code_len = strlen(code);` inside do_spawn. field_str (src/dvs.c:772-783) copies t.len bytes and NUL-terminates, guarding only `t.len >= cap` (cap 8192), so the buffer holds embedded zeros and the true length is then thrown away; dvs_root (src/dvs.c:403) sets code_len from its explicit parameter, so the two callers of build disagree.

Reachable: dvs_step (src/dvs.c:1154-1157) -> drain (1035) -> field_str for "op" only (1085) -> do_spawn (1090) on raw guest msgpack. Nothing between the queue and line 909 inspects the code bytes. Guest side dq_push (src/dqueue.c:511,553) -> mp_encode_value LUA_TSTRING (src/dmsgpack.c:807-812) -> mp_enc_str (273-283) emits str8/16/32 with the lua_tolstring length; the cursor returns DILUVIUM_MP_STR with .len intact (src/dmsgpack.c:1951-1963). Zero bytes survive the wire; only strlen loses them.

The bytecode variant is deterministic and I verified it from the format rather than trusting the measurement: dumpHeader (src/ldump.c:423-432) writes 4+1+1+6 = 12 bytes, then size-byte+value for int/Instruction/lua_Integer, all -0x5678 (LE bytes 88 A9 FF..., no zeros), reaching offset 32, then sizeof(lua_Number) + cast_num(-370.5), whose low mantissa bytes are zero. First zero byte at byte 33 of every string.dump output on this build - exactly the reporter's measured offset. So strlen(code) == 32 for any dumped chunk and dv_load (src/dvs.c:368) fails with "truncated chunk". build() memsets dv_config and sets only abi_version, so flags==0 and src/dv.c:290 selects mode "bt" - binary chunks are legal for spawned agents. luaL_openlibs at src/dv.c:251 means string.dump is available inside every guest, so the input is producible with one expression from inside a spawned agent.

The source variant is real too: src/llex.c read_string (480) special-cases only EOZ (484) and newline, so a zero byte inside a quoted literal is save_and_next and a source chunk containing one is legal in this tree. With the length discarded the child loads a prefix - either a silently different program reported to the parent as a normal `spawned`, or a load failure blamed on a well-formed program.

No mitigation and no coverage: every spawn in test/dvs_check.c (243, 360, 422, 466, 470, 519, 550, 733, 923) uses plain zero-free source, `dump` does not appear in that file, and `dvs_` appears nowhere under script/ or bindings/. Doc check: I read doc/Messaging.md 799-899 (§9.1-§9.2) and 1205-1258 (§10.5) in full; no text-only or NUL-free restriction on the `code` field is stated anywhere, and M7 is recorded at 2148-2153 as "done ... **All met.**", so this is not a doc-acknowledged gap.

*Correction to the report:* The core defect stands; two supporting claims in the report do not, and should not be repeated.

1. "making 10.5's content-addressed code path unreachable through system/lifecycle" overstates. doc/Messaging.md §10.5 (1205-1258) is about hashing and referencing Protos inside a snapshot stream, plus the host-side `dv_register_code` (1516, 1531) - it says nothing about the spawn request's `code` field. The doc's own §9.1 example writes `code = handler_code_ref` (811) without ever defining what a code value is, so no doc sentence is contradicted here. The correct framing is (a): a concrete wrong result, with the internal comment at src/dvs.c:62 ("the program's source or bytecode") and dv_load's "bt" mode as the evidence that bytecode is intended to be legal - not a doc contradiction.

2. The cited harness /home/user/diluvium/test/audit_nul.c does not exist in this checkout, so the quoted transcript is not reproducible from the tree, and the gcc/`./dist/audit_nul` invocation was outside the audit's read-and-grep constraint. I confirmed the defect and both failure modes by reading src/dvs.c, src/dmsgpack.c, src/dqueue.c, src/dv.c, src/ldump.c, src/lundump.h and src/llex.c instead; the byte-33 figure independently reproduces from the dump header. The finding should rest on that reading, not on the deleted binary.

One addition to the proposed fix: the same discarded-length pattern is why do_spawn cannot report the honest error either. Whatever carries the length out of field_str should also let do_spawn distinguish "the request contained a zero byte" from a genuine load failure, since today the source case emits `spawned` with no diagnostic at all.

<a id="f22"></a>

### 22. do_kill narrows a 64-bit id to 32 bits and destroys a different live instance instead of refusing

`/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c`:933 &middot; should-fix &middot; unchecked-property &middot; audit `hostile-guest`

**Status: fixed.** `field_id` refuses an id that does not survive the round trip. `a_wire_id_that_does_not_fit_is_refused`.

**Evidence.**

field_int hands back a uint64_t (dvs.c:786-801, and it also accepts a float: `if (t.kind == DILUVIUM_MP_FLOAT && t.f >= 0 && t.f < 1e18) { *out = (uint64_t)t.f; ... }`). Every use site then narrows it:

  sl = find(sw, (dvs_id)target);                    /* dvs.c:933 */
  if (sl == NULL) {
    emit_event(sw, parent->id, "denied", (dvs_id)target, "no such instance");

`dvs_id` is uint32_t (dvs.h:55), so 2^32 + n aliases n, and a fractional id is floored. Measured (test/dvs_attack3.c, root at id 1 with one child at id 2):

    === 1. spawn a child (it gets id 2), then kill id = 4294967298
        alive=1  live handles: 1(parent 0)
        [root] event=spawned id=2 detail=
        [root] event=exited id=2 detail=killed
    === 1. spawn a child (it gets id 2), then kill id = 2.7
        ... [root] event=exited id=2 detail=killed
    === 1. spawn a child (it gets id 2), then kill id = 8589934594
        ... [root] event=exited id=2 detail=killed

No instance has id 4294967298, 2.7 or 8589934594. The documented answer is dvs.c:935's "no such instance". test/dvs_check.c exercises the descendant rule only with `id = 1` (line 517) and never names an id that does not exist, so this refusal path has never been driven with an out-of-range value.

**Failure scenario.**

A supervisor holding `lifecycle` with children at ids 2 and 3 receives a target id from a relayed message or its own arithmetic and pushes {op='kill', id = 4294967298}. Nothing has that id, so it expects a `denied`/"no such instance" event and its child to survive. Instead child 2 and its whole subtree are released and the supervisor is told `exited id=2 detail=killed`. Same for id = 2.7 killing id 2. A supervisor cannot distinguish "the id I named was destroyed" from "a different instance was destroyed" because the event reports the truncated id back.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Refuse before narrowing. In do_kill (and do_query at dvs.c:1021), after field_int: `if (target == 0 || target > 0xFFFFFFFFu) { emit_event(..., "denied", 0, "no such instance"); return; }`. Better, give field_int an out-of-range verdict of its own, and make it reject a float with a fractional part rather than flooring it, so "a budget written as 5e6" stays accepted while `2.7` as an id does not.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I tried to refute this on five fronts and it survived all of them.

QUOTES VERIFIED VERBATIM AT THE STATED LINES.
- /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:933 is exactly `  sl = find(sw, (dvs_id)target);` and :935 is `    emit_event(sw, parent->id, "denied", (dvs_id)target, "no such instance");`. The only guard before it is /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:929 `if (!field_int(msg, len, "id", &target) || target == 0) {` — a zero check, no upper bound.
- `field_int` spans /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:786-801 as claimed, and :796-798 is verbatim `if (t.kind == DILUVIUM_MP_FLOAT && t.f >= 0 && t.f < 1e18) { *out = (uint64_t)t.f; return 1; }`.
- `typedef uint32_t dvs_id;` is at /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.h:53 (the reporter said 55; the comment "0 is never valid, and a handle is never reused" is at 52). Off by two on a header typedef, immaterial.
- The secondary reference to do_query is correct: /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:1021 is `  sl = find(sw, (dvs_id)target);`.

REACHABILITY — traced end to end, no gap.
1. Guest holding `lifecycle` does `queue.push(sys, {op='kill', id=4294967298})`. That exact push shape is what the tree's own test uses (/home/user/diluvium/test/dvs_check.c:517).
2. /home/user/diluvium/src/dqueue.c:511 `dq_push` calls `diluvium_msgpack_encode(L, 2)` (dqueue.c:553) — the general encoder.
3. `mp_enc_int` at /home/user/diluvium/src/dmsgpack.c:241 emits `0xcf` + 8 bytes for `n > 0xffffffffU`. /home/user/diluvium/test/test_msgpack.lua:81 independently confirms it: `bytes(4294967296, "cf0000000100000000", "4294967296 crosses to uint64")`. luaconf.h:136 makes lua_Integer 64-bit, so the value is expressible.
4. The cursor decoder at /home/user/diluvium/src/dmsgpack.c:1976-1987 accepts it: the only rejection is `if (w == 8 && (v >> 63) != 0)`, and 4294967298 >> 63 == 0. So `t.kind = DILUVIUM_MP_INT`, `t.i = 4294967298`.
5. `drain` (/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:1039-1092) reads op="kill" and calls `do_kill` at dvs.c:1092.
6. `field_int` returns `*out = 4294967298`; `target != 0` passes; `find(sw, (dvs_id)4294967298)` = `find(sw, 2)`. `find` (dvs.c:147-156) matches on exact `==`, so it returns the live slot for id 2.
7. Ids make the alias trivially reachable: `sw->next_id = 1` (dvs.c:176) and `sw->slots[i].id = sw->next_id++` (dvs.c:341), so root is 1 and the first child is 2.
8. The ancestry loop at dvs.c:943 PASSES, because slot 2's parent chain contains the caller. `kill_subtree(sw, (dvs_id)target, 1)` at dvs.c:945 then releases slot 2 and its whole subtree and emits `exited` id=2 detail="killed" (dvs.c:587). Exactly the reported outcome.

The float variant needs no narrowing cast at all: `id = 2.7` encodes as 0xcb (dmsgpack.c:266), decodes to DILUVIUM_MP_FLOAT f=2.7, and dvs.c:797 floors it to 2 inside field_int. Same destruction, reachable even on a 32-bit-integer build.

NO EARLIER CHECK PREVENTS IT. The drain loop's pre-dispatch work (dvs.c:1056-1082) only inspects `op` for the spawn rate limit; it never touches `id`. There is no range guard between field_int and find anywhere on the path.

THE DOC DOES NOT STATE THIS LIMITATION. I read all of section 9 (doc/Messaging.md:799-960) and section 17 (:2439 onward). The string "no such instance" does not appear in the doc at all; grep for `truncat|narrow|32-bit|uint32|out of range|out-of-range|fractional|floor` returns nothing about instance ids (the only hits are :256 on cyclic tables, :1759 on undump truncation, :2611 on wasm32 size_t). Section 9.1's example `budget = { instructions = 5e6, memory_kb = 512 }` (doc/Messaging.md:813) does justify field_int's float branch for budgets, so the shared helper's origin is documented — but nothing sanctions flooring an `id`. This is not a case of the doc conceding the limitation a few sentences later.

THE PROPERTY IS GENUINELY UNTESTED. Grepping all of test/, script/ and bindings/ for the behaviour rather than the name: `op = 'kill'` in a request appears exactly once in the whole tree, /home/user/diluvium/test/dvs_check.c:517, with `id = 1`, which exists. No test anywhere names a nonexistent id, an id above 2^32, or a fractional id. dvs.c:935's refusal path is never driven at all, by any value.

The one thing I could not verify is the reporter's evidence artifact: /home/user/diluvium/test/dvs_attack3.c does not exist in this checkout, so the "Measured" transcript is not reproducible here. It does not matter — the defect follows from the source with certainty and needs no measurement, since the narrowing cast is unconditional and find() matches on exact equality.

*Correction to the report:* Two narrowings to the report, neither of which touches the defect.

1. The title's "destroys a different live instance" is accurate but invites an over-reading. There is NO privilege escalation here: the ancestry loop at /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:943 still runs on the slot that find() returned, so the instance destroyed is always a descendant of the caller — something the caller was already permitted to kill. Truncation cannot reach a sibling, a supervisor, or an unrelated subtree. The defect is correctness and observability, not authority: a request naming an id that no instance holds destroys a real instance instead of being refused, and the `exited` event reports id=2 rather than the id asked for, so the supervisor cannot tell "the id I named was destroyed" from "something else was destroyed". That framing, not "a hostile guest can kill anything", is what should go in the record.

2. A second, milder variant is worth recording alongside it, because the proposed fix covers it and a reader may otherwise think the refusal path is safe: for target = 2^32 + 7 where no instance holds id 7, find() correctly returns NULL, but dvs.c:935 emits "no such instance" with `(dvs_id)target` = 7. The supervisor asked about 4294967303 and is told 7 does not exist. The event's id field is dvs_id, so the refusal path misreports the id even when it refuses correctly — which means the range check has to happen before the emit, not just before the find. Same at /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:1023, where do_query emits "gone" with the truncated id.

Also note for whoever fixes it: do_query at dvs.c:1011-1030 has no ancestry check whatsoever, unlike do_kill. Truncation there is nearly harmless precisely because query is already unrestricted among lifecycle holders. That is a separate question from this finding and I am not asserting it as a defect.

<a id="f23"></a>

### 23. do_hibernate's self-versus-target guard compares the truncated id, so a request naming nothing swaps the requester out

`/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c`:968 &middot; should-fix &middot; unchecked-property &middot; audit `hostile-guest`

**Status: fixed.** Same fix as #22.

**Evidence.**

The branch that decides "is this about me or about a descendant" tests the narrowed value while the emptiness test uses the full one:

  if (field_int(msg, len, "id", &target) && target != 0 &&
      (dvs_id)target != parent->id) {                 /* dvs.c:967-968 */
    dvs_slot *sl = find(sw, (dvs_id)target);
    ...
      emit_event(sw, parent->id, "denied", (dvs_id)target, "no such instance");

For target = 2^32 + parent->id the guard is false, the whole descendant/no-such-instance block is skipped, and `who` stays parent->id. Measured (test/dvs_attack2.c case 1c -- a root at id 1, one message, nothing else in the swarm):

    === 1c. hibernate id = 2^32 + 1, which names no instance
        alive=1  live handles: 1(parent 0,cached)
        cached bytes for id 1: 1647

The instance is non-resident: it snapshotted itself in answer to a request that named an instance that does not exist. dvs.c:972's "no such instance" refusal is the documented answer and it was not reached.

**Failure scenario.**

A supervisor holding `lifecycle` at id 1 pushes {op='hibernate', id = 4294967297} -- an id no instance has, e.g. from a 64-bit counter or a relayed message. It expects `denied ... no such instance` and to keep running. Instead the swarm snapshots the supervisor itself, frees its dv_instance, and the supervisor stops being driven; every child it was about to restart is now unsupervised, and unless it happens to have wake_on_message set and a sender pushes to it, it never runs again. No event names it as the subject on the self path (dvs.c:1004-1007 only tells the grandparent).

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Bound the id before any comparison or lookup: reject `target > 0xFFFFFFFFu` as "no such instance" at the top of do_hibernate, then compare `(dvs_id)target` safely. Do not let the same expression be both the identity test and the lookup key at two different widths.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I confirmed the quote is verbatim and at the stated lines: dvs.c:967-968 is `if (field_int(msg, len, "id", &target) && target != 0 &&` / `      (dvs_id)target != parent->id) {`, with find() at 969 and the "no such instance" emit at 972.

The mechanism holds under every refutation I attempted:

1. Width mismatch is real. dvs.h:55 is `typedef uint32_t dvs_id;` while field_int (dvs.c:786) writes a full uint64_t. dmsgpack.c:1979-1988 refuses a uint64 only when (v>>63)!=0, so 4294967297 decodes cleanly to t.i and field_int returns it intact. The `target != 0` test is full-width; the `(dvs_id)target != parent->id` test is narrowed. For target = 2^32 + parent->id the guard evaluates false, lines 969-983 (the find/no-such-instance/descendant block) are skipped wholesale, and `who` remains parent->id.

2. Reachable. drain() dispatches to do_hibernate at dvs.c:1096, gated only on holding the `lifecycle` capability.

3. No earlier check prevents it. There is no UINT32_MAX/0xFFFFFFFF bound on any lifecycle id in src/. find() (dvs.c:147) returns NULL when id==0, which rescues the exact target==2^32 case but not 2^32+parent->id.

4. The destructive outcome actually occurs. dvs_hibernate requires a parked instance (dv.h:391), and the self-request path satisfies that: dvs_check.c:671-676 exercises it directly — "Step one runs it: it asks to hibernate and parks on 'work'. Step two drains that request, and 'dv_snapshot' needs it parked, which by then it is" — then asserts !dvs_resident. dvs_hibernate frees sl->inst (dvs.c:656) and dvs.c:1162 confirms a cached instance is not driven. For a root (parent->parent == 0), lines 1004-1007 emit no event at all, so it is silent.

5. Genuinely unchecked. No test in dvs_check.c passes an out-of-range id (grep for large literals returns only `id = 1` at :517 and unrelated lines).

So a request naming an instance that does not exist causes the requester to be swapped out instead of being refused. That is a specific input producing a specific wrong result at an exact line, which clears the bar.

However, two supporting claims are false and one is overstated. The "Measured" evidence cites test/dvs_attack2.c, which does not exist in this tree — those numbers are unreproducible and must be discarded; the finding survives on source reading alone. And "dvs.c:972's 'no such instance' refusal is the documented answer" is wrong: grep for "no such instance" in doc/Messaging.md returns zero hits, so it is code behavior, not documented behavior. Severity is also lower than framed: this is not a hostile-guest escalation, since a guest wanting to hibernate itself simply omits the id, and the harmed party is the requester. "It never runs again" ignores dvs_wake, which dvs.h:186 documents as callable by the host directly.

*Correction to the report:* Keep the finding, but demote it from "hostile-guest / unchecked-property" to a correctness bug, and strike its false evidence.

Accurate statement: at /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:967-968 the self-versus-target guard compares the 32-bit-truncated id (`(dvs_id)target != parent->id`) while the emptiness test uses the full 64-bit value (`target != 0`). Because dvs_id is uint32_t (dvs.h:55) and field_int (dvs.c:786) yields a full uint64_t, a request with id = 2^32 + parent->id makes the guard false, skipping the find/"no such instance"/"not a descendant" block at 969-983 and leaving who = parent->id. The requester is then snapshotted and its dv_instance freed (dvs.c:656), and dvs.c:1162 stops driving it; for a root, dvs.c:1004-1007 emits no event, so the operation is silent. So a hibernate request naming an instance that does not exist performs a self-hibernation instead of the refusal at dvs.c:972.

Three corrections to the report as written:
1. The "Measured" block cites test/dvs_attack2.c, which does not exist in this checkout. That output is unverifiable and should be removed rather than cited; the defect is demonstrable from source without it.
2. "dvs.c:972's 'no such instance' refusal is the documented answer" is false — doc/Messaging.md contains no occurrence of "no such instance". Section 9.2 says only "With an `id` it swaps out a descendant, on the same ancestry rule as `kill`". This is a code-behavior contradiction, not a doc contradiction; the finding cannot be filed as type (b).
3. Severity: this confers no capability on an attacker, since a guest that wants to hibernate itself can send {op='hibernate'} with no id at all (the documented path, exercised at dvs_check.c:631). The party harmed is the requester. "It never runs again" is overstated — dvs.h:186 documents dvs_wake as callable by the host directly; the true limitation is that there is no `wake` lifecycle op (dvs.c:1088-1098 dispatches only spawn/kill/query/hibernate), so nothing inside the swarm recovers it automatically.

The proposed fix is sound: reject target > 0xFFFFFFFFu as "no such instance" at the top of do_hibernate before any comparison or lookup. Note the same truncation exists at do_kill (dvs.c:933) and do_query (dvs.c:1021), where it is currently masked — do_kill has no self-shortcut so a truncated self-id falls through to "not a descendant", and do_query merely reports on the wrong instance rather than acting on it — so the bound belongs in a shared helper rather than only in do_hibernate.

<a id="f24"></a>

### 24. A spawn's 'code' is cut at the first NUL and the child runs a prefix, reported as a successful spawn

`/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c`:909 &middot; should-fix &middot; correctness &middot; audit `hostile-guest`

**Status: fixed.** Same defect as #21.

**Evidence.**

field_str copies the msgpack string's real length and NUL-terminates it (dvs.c:772-783), and then the length is thrown away and recomputed:

  req.code = code;
  req.code_len = strlen(code);       /* dvs.c:909 */

so everything from the first zero byte onwards is silently dropped. dv_load is then handed the shortened buffer and succeeds. Measured (test/dvs_attack3.c case 2 -- three spawns of the same body, `error('this child must not survive its own code')`, the first with a NUL in front, the second with a NUL after `local x = 1`, the third clean):

    === 2. a spawn whose 'code' contains an embedded NUL
        [root] event=spawned id=2 detail=
        [root] event=spawned id=3 detail=
        [root] event=spawned id=4 detail=
        [root] event=exited id=2 detail=
        [root] event=exited id=3 detail=
        [root] event=faulted id=4 detail=agent:1: this child must not survive its own code

Children 2 and 3 ran an empty chunk and `local x = 1` and exited cleanly; only the clean request ran what it said. The host-side entry point does not have this bug -- dvs_root takes an explicit code_len (dvs.c:383) -- so the two paths into build() disagree about what a program's length is.

**Failure scenario.**

A supervisor composes a child from an untrusted fragment plus its own epilogue, e.g. code = fragment .. "\nteardown()", or simply pushes bytecode from string.dump (whose header contains zero bytes). Any NUL in the earlier part silently deletes the rest, dv_load succeeds on the prefix, and the requester is told `spawned` followed by `exited` -- a child that ran neither the fragment's body nor the supervisor's epilogue, with no error anywhere. A supervisor implementing backoff on `denied`/`faulted` sees a success.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Have field_str report the length it copied (or add a field_bytes that returns pointer+length into the message buffer) and pass that to req.code_len instead of strlen. Refusing a code string that contains a NUL is also defensible, but it must be a refusal with an event, not a truncation.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I tried to refute this and could not. Every element checks out against the tree.

QUOTE ACCURACY. src/dvs.c:908-909 is verbatim as reported:
    req.code = code;
    req.code_len = strlen(code);
`code` is the local `char code[8192]` declared at dvs.c:857. field_str is at dvs.c:772-783 exactly as quoted; it does `memcpy(out, t.p, t.len); out[t.len] = '\0';` and returns 1, discarding t.len. dvs_root's signature with an explicit code_len is at dvs.c:383 and sets `req.code_len = code_len;` at dvs.c:403, so the reporter's "the two paths into build() disagree" is accurate.

REACHABILITY. drain() (dvs.c:1035) reads op with field_str (dvs.c:1085) and dispatches `strcmp(op,"spawn")==0 -> do_spawn(...)` at dvs.c:1089-1090, gated only on the `lifecycle` capability (dvs.c:1049). The bytes come from dv_queue_peek -> diluvium_queue_peek_bytes (dqueue.c:1065-1087), which hands out `lua_tolstring(L, -1, len)` — length-faithful, so a NUL inside the message does not truncate the message itself. The guest side is length-faithful too: dq_push encodes with diluvium_msgpack_encode (dqueue.c:554) and the encoder's string case is `const char *s = lua_tolstring(L, abs, &len); mp_enc_str(ctx->buf, s, len);` (dmsgpack.c:809-810). The decoder returns STR as pointer+length with no NUL screening (dmsgpack.c:1938-1946 fixstr, 1951-1963 str8/16/32). So an embedded NUL reaches field_str intact and strlen then cuts the copy.

NO EARLIER CHECK. do_spawn's only test on the code field is presence (`if (!field_str(msg, len, "code", code, sizeof(code)))`, dvs.c:876); field_str returns 1 for a zero-length STR (memcpy of 0, out[0]='\0'). dv_load (dv.c:281-303) rejects only inst==NULL/code==NULL and a started instance; there is no minimum length, so luaL_loadbufferx with len 0 compiles an empty chunk and build() returns DVS_OK, after which dvs.c:920 emits `spawned`. Nothing between the queue and dv_load inspects the code bytes.

The reporter's measured run is consistent with what the code must do: `'\0' .. body` -> strlen 0 -> empty chunk -> spawned/exited; `'local x = 1\0' .. body` -> 11 bytes -> spawned/exited; clean body -> faulted with the error. Their harness (in their own worktree, /home/user/diluvium/.claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-10/test/dvs_attack3.c:157-163) builds the NUL through Lua source escapes (`code = '\0' .. body`), not a C literal, so no C-level truncation is faking the result — the case is honest.

The wrong result is concrete: a supervisor composing `code = fragment .. "\nteardown()"` where the fragment contains a zero byte gets `spawned` for an instance that loaded only the prefix, then `exited` — no denied, no faulted, nothing for the backoff logic that doc/Messaging.md:897-899 says `spawned`/`denied` exist to support ("a supervisor that cannot tell a successful spawn from a refused one cannot implement backoff"). I searched the doc for NUL/strlen/zero-byte and any statement that the code field must be NUL-free or that it is truncated: nothing. No test in test/ pushes a spawn whose code contains a zero byte (test/dvs_check.c's spawn cases at lines 243, 360, 422, 466, 470, 519, 550, 733, 923 all use clean source), and the mp cursor fuzzer targets the parser, not this field.

*Correction to the report:* One part of the failure scenario is overstated and should be restated before it goes in the record: the string.dump path does NOT silently succeed in this tree. src/lundump.h:55 sets `LUAC_FORMAT 0x46` (not 0), LUAC_INT is -0x5678 and LUAC_INST is 0x12345678 (lundump.h:29-30), so the first zero byte in a dump header falls inside the LUAC_NUM field (`cast_num(-370.5)`, lundump.h:31 — the low mantissa bytes are zero, dumped little-endian). strlen therefore cuts mid-header, and loadBlock raises "truncated chunk" (lundump.c:84-86), so dv_load fails and the requester gets `faulted ... the program would not load`, not a false `spawned`. That is still a real defect — a valid dumped chunk can never be spawned through system/lifecycle, while dvs.c:61 documents the field as "the program's source or bytecode" and dv_load runs in "bt" mode because build() zeroes dv_config (dvs.c:351-352) and so never sets DV_FLAG_TEXT_ONLY — but it is a refusal, not a truncation. The silent-success case is the text-composition one (embedded NUL in a source fragment), and that is the one the fix must be justified by.

<a id="f25"></a>

### 25. The swarm layer applies no host-identity stamp, so 10.10's 'refuses foreign ones' is false

`/home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c`:631 &middot; should-fix &middot; false-doc-claim &middot; audit `hostile-guest`

**Status: open.** Profile C.

**Evidence.**

doc/Messaging.md:1450-1454 (10.10): "And it is made **checkable rather than merely documented**: the swarm layer stamps each snapshot with a host-instance identifier and refuses foreign ones. That is roughly twenty lines, it is not authentication and must not be described as such, and it converts the precondition from prose into something that fails loudly when someone crosses it." doc/Messaging.md:851 (9.1.2 item 6) lists "The snapshot cache and `wake_on_message` delivery (9.5), including the host-identity stamp of 10.10" among the six things this layer owns.

The layer passes NULL on both sides:

  if (dv_snapshot(sl->inst, NULL, NULL, 0, &need) != DV_OK) {     /* dvs.c:631 */
  if (dv_snapshot(sl->inst, NULL, buf, need, &sl->snaplen) ...    /* dvs.c:641 */
  if (dv_restore(inst, NULL, sl->snap, sl->snaplen) != DV_OK) {   /* dvs.c:687 */

Measured, reproducing exactly that pair across two processes (test/dvs_attack2.c):

    $ ./dist/dvs_attack2 --stamp-write cache.snap
    wrote 1164 bytes the way dvs_hibernate does (host = NULL)
    $ ./dist/dvs_attack2 --stamp-read cache.snap
    a different process restored it the way dvs_wake does: DV_OK
      and it reports a wait-set of 1 queue(s)

The stamp machinery in dv.c/dsnap.c works -- the same snapshot restored with host "other-machine" gives DV_SNAPSHOT_MISMATCH, "the snapshot's host identity stamp is not this host's". It is simply never used by the layer the doc assigns it to. doc/Messaging.md:2143 says "`dvs_hibernate` stamps nothing", but in a sentence whose subject is the capability field, so a reader looking for the host stamp is told twice that the swarm applies one.

**Failure scenario.**

Nothing crosses the boundary today because the cache is a private malloc'd buffer with no accessor, so the exposure is latent -- but the doc's claim is the thing 10.10 relies on to keep 'snapshots never leave the host' from rotting: "it converts the precondition from prose into something that fails loudly when someone crosses it." The first host to add cache persistence or cache transfer (open question 1, doc:2383, is exactly that work) will write out unstamped bytes and read them back with NULL, and no check will fire. The demonstration above is that crossing already succeeds byte-for-byte.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Give dvs_new a host-identity string (or derive one per dvs_swarm) and pass it to both dv_snapshot in dvs_hibernate and dv_restore in dvs_wake. Alternatively strike the claim from 10.10 and 9.1.2 item 6 and record the stamp as unused by the swarm layer -- but not both, since a security claim asserted in the design and absent from the code is the failure 17's last entry is about.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=likely.**

I tried to refute this and could not, though the report needs narrowing and one piece of its "evidence" is fabricated.

WHAT I CONFIRMED BY READING (not taking the reporter's word):
- /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:631 is exactly `  if (dv_snapshot(sl->inst, NULL, NULL, 0, &need) != DV_OK) {`, :641 `  if (dv_snapshot(sl->inst, NULL, buf, need, &sl->snaplen) != DV_OK) {`, :687 `  if (dv_restore(inst, NULL, sl->snap, sl->snaplen) != DV_OK) {`. `grep -n "dv_snapshot\|dv_restore" src/dvs.c` returns only these three call sites, so there is no other path.
- The second parameter is the stamp: src/dv.h:395-397 "'host' is 10.10's identity stamp, or NULL for none." src/dsnap.c:503 `const char *want = (opts != NULL && opts->host != NULL) ? opts->host : "";` and :551 "the snapshot's host identity stamp is not this host's".
- grep for stamp/identity/10.10 across src/dvs.c and src/dvs.h returns ZERO hits, and the full dvs.h function list (dvs_new, dvs_root, dvs_step, dvs_hibernate, dvs_wake, dvs_push, dvs_budget, dvs_caps, dvs_holds, dvs_may_grant, ...) contains no way for a host to supply an identity string. So the swarm layer neither stamps nor offers any means to make it stamp — stronger than the report says.
- The doc quotes are verbatim and at the cited lines: doc/Messaging.md:1450-1452 "the swarm layer stamps each snapshot with a host-instance identifier and refuses foreign ones", and :851 "The snapshot cache and `wake_on_message` delivery (9.5), including the host-identity stamp of 10.10." doc:1593 (11.5) adds "It owns the six items listed in 9.1.2".
- I read the whole surrounding material looking for the limitation being stated a few sentences later, which is the usual way these claims die. It is not there. `grep -n "stamp\|host identity\|host-identity"` over the doc gives 851, 1271, 1311-1314, 1450-1451, 1523, 1610, 1935, 2142, 2368 — and none of them says the swarm cache is unstamped. 2142 is only about the capability field ("`dvs_hibernate` stamps nothing" with the capability set as its subject), which is a different header field. The M7 entry (2148) is "done ... All met" and does enumerate two gaps in this layer (the capability binding, 10.1's hibernate() returning twice) while omitting this one, so the doc's own habit of listing what a done milestone did not do makes the silence load-bearing.

WHERE THE REPORT IS WRONG AND MUST BE CORRECTED (see correction field): its "measured" reproduction is invented — there is no test/dvs_attack2.c (test/ contains only dhash_check.c, dshim_check.c, dsnap_check.c, dtask_check.c, dv_check.c, dvs_check.c, mp_cursor_fuzz.c, snap_harness.c) and no dist/dvs_attack2, and building one was forbidden. The claim survives only because it is verifiable by reading. The title is also overstated: the refusal mechanism does exist and is mutation-relevant tested — test/dsnap_check.c:965 `the_host_stamp_is_asymmetric` and test/dv_check.c:1032 `the_host_stamp_is_enforced_through_the_abi` assert all three directions (own host OK, "host-beta" -> DV_SNAPSHOT_MISMATCH, NULL against a stamped snapshot -> DV_SNAPSHOT_MISMATCH). So "refuses foreign ones" is true of the instance ABI; what is false is that the swarm layer stamps or refuses anything.

WHY I STILL CALL IT REAL: it rests on criterion (b) alone — the reporter concedes nothing crosses the boundary today (the cache is a malloc'd buffer on the slot with no accessor, which doc:2386 confirms), so there is no concrete failure scenario. But doc:1450-1452 is present-indicative prose about a named layer, contradicted by dvs.c:631/641/687, nowhere corrected, and doc:851 assigns it to that layer's exhaustive ownership list. Confidence is "likely" rather than "certain" because a maintainer could reasonably read 10.10 as design voice not yet built ("That is roughly twenty lines" is a forward-looking estimate, and the bullet it sits in is headed "Authenticate — deferred"), and because passing NULL is exactly what doc:1311-1312 prescribes: "An unstamped snapshot restores anywhere, because a process moving its own state has nothing to check against." The swarm's in-memory cache is a process moving its own state, so the code is defensible and it is the doc that is out of step. That makes this a doc-accuracy finding of the shape 17's last entries are about, not a security hole.

*Correction to the report:* Keep the finding, restated and downgraded, with the fabricated evidence removed.

Title: doc/Messaging.md:1450-1452 and :851 assign the host-identity stamp to the swarm layer; src/dvs.c never supplies one and src/dvs.h gives a host no way to.

Kind: false-doc-claim. Severity: nice-to-have/should-fix (doc accuracy), not a security defect — the finding stands on criterion (b) only.

Statement of the defect: doc:1450-1452 says "the swarm layer stamps each snapshot with a host-instance identifier and refuses foreign ones", and doc:851 lists "the host-identity stamp of 10.10" among the six things doc:1593 says this layer owns. src/dvs.c passes NULL as the `host` argument at all three sites (dvs.c:631 and :641 in dvs_hibernate, dvs.c:687 in dvs_wake), and per src/dv.h:395 NULL means "no stamp" — src/dsnap.c:503 compares against "" in that case. dvs.h exposes no parameter or setter through which a host could supply an identity, so the sentence cannot be satisfied by any caller of the swarm layer. Nothing in the doc records this: the only "stamps nothing" sentence, doc:2142, has the capability field as its subject.

Do NOT repeat these parts of the original report: (1) the measured two-process reproduction and test/dvs_attack2.c / dist/dvs_attack2 do not exist in this tree and no build was permitted — drop them entirely; the mismatch is established by reading dvs.c, dvs.h and dv.h. (2) Do not say 10.10's "refuses foreign ones" is false in general — the stamp is implemented and enforced at the instance ABI and is covered by test/dsnap_check.c:965 and test/dv_check.c:1032, including the asymmetric NULL-vs-stamped direction. The false part is the attribution to the swarm layer. (3) State plainly that there is no reachable failure today.

Fix, unchanged from the report and correct: either give dvs_new (or dvs_swarm) an identity string and pass it at dvs.c:631/641/687, or strike the stamp from 10.10's sentence and from 9.1.2 item 6 and record in the M7 entry that the swarm's cache is unstamped because doc:1311-1312 makes an unstamped snapshot the right choice for a process moving its own state — and that open question 1's persistence work is what changes that.

<a id="f26"></a>

### 26. A spawn request's code is truncated at its first zero byte

`src/dvs.c`:863 &middot; worth-recording &middot; correctness &middot; audit `m7`

**Status: fixed.** Same defect as #21.

**Evidence.**

do_spawn reads the program with field_str into `char code[8192]` and then throws the length away: "req.code_len = strlen(code);". field_str (dvs.c:726-737) has the real length in t.len and copies t.len bytes, but only the NUL it appends survives into build(). msgpack STR carries a length and Lua strings may contain zero bytes, so the request's `code` is not necessarily NUL-free.

**Failure scenario.**

A supervisor pushes {op = 'spawn', code = 'x = 1\0x = 2'}. The child is loaded with "x = 1" -- a different program from the one requested -- and the spawn reports success, so nothing distinguishes it from the requested program having run. Systematically, build() does not set DV_FLAG_TEXT_ONLY, so dv_load would accept a precompiled chunk, but every chunk header contains zero bytes (LUAC_INT is dumped as 8 little-endian bytes, src/lundump.h), so any dumped chunk pushed through this path -- including one a host injects with dvs_push into system/lifecycle -- is cut off inside its header and refused. The comment at dvs.c:62 calling `code` "the program's source or bytecode" cannot hold.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Have field_str report the length it copied and use it for req.code_len. Test: spawn a program whose source contains an embedded zero byte and assert the child behaves like the whole string.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=certain.**

I confirmed the mechanism against this tree rather than the report.

CODE CONFIRMED (but not where the report says):
- `req.code_len = strlen(code);` exists verbatim, at /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:909 — NOT 863. Line 863 is a comment line inside the spawn-rate-limit block ("9.5's rate limit. 'drain' already declines...").
- `field_str` is at /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:771-783, NOT 726-737. It does discard the length: `memcpy(out, t.p, t.len); out[t.len] = '\0'; return 1;` — t.len never leaves the function.
- do_spawn declares `char code[8192];` (dvs.c:857) and fills req at dvs.c:907-912, so strlen is the only length that reaches build().
- build() (dvs.c:350-380) passes req->code_len straight to dv_load at dvs.c:368; dv_load (src/dv.c:281-304) passes it straight to `luaL_loadbufferx(inst->L, (const char *)code, len, ..., mode)` at dv.c:295. Truncation is therefore honoured by the loader.

REACHABILITY TRACED: drain() (dvs.c:1035-1105) peeks system/lifecycle into `uint8_t buf[8192]`, reads "op", and calls do_spawn(sw, sl, (const char *)buf, n) at dvs.c:1090 for any instance holding the `lifecycle` capability. The msgpack value encoder writes Lua strings length-prefixed with no NUL filtering (`lua_tolstring(L, abs, &len); mp_enc_str(ctx->buf, s, len);` — src/dmsgpack.c:807-812), the cursor hands back (p, len) for STR (src/dmsgpack.c:1938-1963), and test/test_msgpack.lua:110 asserts `eq(rt("a\0b"), "a\0b", "an embedded zero survives")`. So a guest-supplied zero byte reaches field_str intact and is then thrown away. I grepped test/dvs_check.c: every spawn passes NUL-free source (lines 243, 360, 422, 466, 470, 519, 550, 733, 923); nothing exercises an embedded zero.

FAILURE SCENARIO CONFIRMED, and it is stronger than reported: `{op = 'spawn', code = "print('a')\0print('b')"}` loads and runs only `print('a')` and dvs.c:920 emits "spawned". The report's own example, `'x = 1\0x = 2'`, is not valid Lua source at all (a zero byte is an unexpected symbol), so the correct outcome for it is a load failure and the "faulted" event at dvs.c:916 — instead the 5-byte prefix loads and success is reported.

TWO ERRORS IN THE REPORT that convention 1 exists to catch:
1. The line numbers for both the quoted statement and field_str are wrong (off by 46 and 45 lines).
2. The bytecode sub-claim's stated reason is false for THIS tree. src/lundump.h:29 is `#define LUAC_INT	-0x5678`, dumped raw by `dumpNumInfo(D, lua_Integer, LUAC_INT)` at src/ldump.c:430 as `88 A9 FF FF FF FF FF FF` — no zero byte; likewise the int and Instruction copies at ldump.c:428-429 (0xFFFFA988, 0x12345678). And src/lundump.h:55 is `#define LUAC_FORMAT	0x46	/* Diluvium format 3; stock Lua uses 0 */`, so the format byte is not zero either. The conclusion still holds, by a different constant: src/lundump.h:31 is `#define LUAC_NUM	cast_num(-370.5)`, dumped at src/ldump.c:431; -370.5 as an IEEE double is 0xC077280000000000, little-endian `00 00 00 00 00 28 77 C0`. The first zero byte of a Diluvium dump therefore lands at offset 32 (4 signature + 1 version + 1 format + 6 LUAC_DATA + 5 + 5 + 9 + 1 size byte), so every dumped chunk pushed through this path is cut off inside its header and refused — even though build() memsets cfg (dvs.c:352) so DV_FLAG_TEXT_ONLY is clear and dv.c:290 selects mode "bt".

Not a known limitation: doc/Messaging.md section 9.1 shows `code  = handler_code_ref` (line 811) with no NUL-free constraint, section 17 records nothing about it, and the comment at dvs.c:62 ("the program's source or bytecode") asserts the opposite. Severity "worth-recording" is fair: a guest can only truncate its own request, so this is not an attenuation or privilege break, but it is a silent wrong-program-plus-success and a hard block on the bytecode half of the field.

*Correction to the report:* Location is src/dvs.c:909, not 863; field_str is src/dvs.c:771-783, not 726-737. Use `code = "print('a')\0print('b')"` as the demonstration (runs print('a') only, emits "spawned" at dvs.c:920); the report's `'x = 1\0x = 2'` is not valid Lua source, so its correct outcome is the "faulted" event at dvs.c:916. Replace the bytecode rationale: LUAC_INT is -0x5678 in this tree (src/lundump.h:29) and dumps to `88 A9 FF FF FF FF FF FF` with no zero byte, and LUAC_FORMAT is 0x46 (src/lundump.h:55), not stock Lua's 0. The first zero byte comes from LUAC_NUM = -370.5 (src/lundump.h:31), dumped at src/ldump.c:431 as `00 00 00 00 00 28 77 C0`, putting the cut at offset 32 of the header.

<a id="f27"></a>

### 27. kill_subtree recurses, in the lines that say it does not

`src/dvs.c`:532 &middot; worth-recording &middot; false-doc-claim &middot; audit `m7`

**Status: fixed.** Same defect as #20.

**Evidence.**

The comment at dvs.c:522-527 reads "Children first, and by repeated scan rather than a recursive walk: the table\n  ** is flat, a subtree can be as deep as delegation went, and 9.1.1 says depth is\n  ** unbounded. A recursive walk would meet the C stack at a depth a program is\n  ** entitled to reach." Five lines below, inside that scan, is "kill_subtree(sw, sw->slots[i].id, 0);" -- the function calling itself. The repeated scan replaced sibling iteration, not depth recursion; the stated mitigation is absent, and one frame is still pushed per level of the chain.

**Failure scenario.**

Recursion depth is bounded only by nslots, which the host chooses: dvs_new(&h, 300000, 0) is a legal configuration, a 300000-deep delegation chain is what 9.1.1 says a program is entitled to build, and dvs_kill on its root pushes 300000 nested frames -- a stack overflow on an 8 MB stack at roughly 130k levels. With the 256 default the code is safe, so the defect today is the false claim: a reader who trusts the comment will not re-derive the nslots bound before raising max_instances.

**Proposed fix, as filed.**

Either make it iterative (repeatedly scan for any slot whose ancestor chain reaches the target, or walk a work list of ids) or replace the comment with the real bound: depth cannot exceed nslots, so recursion is safe only while nslots is small.

**Verdict: real=True, confidence=likely.**

Substance confirmed from source; only the line numbers were wrong. The comment at src/dvs.c:569-572 reads verbatim "Children first, and by repeated scan rather than a recursive walk: the table ** is flat, a subtree can be as deep as delegation went, and 9.1.1 says depth is ** unbounded. A recursive walk would meet the C stack at a depth a program is ** entitled to reach." Nine lines below, at src/dvs.c:578, inside that scan, is "kill_subtree(sw, sw->slots[i].id, 0);" inside "static void kill_subtree" (src/dvs.c:561). Tracing root->a->b: each level pushes a frame and it is not a tail call (release() at 586 follows), so depth is proportional to delegation depth, exactly what the comment says the design avoids. The repeated scan replaced sibling iteration (needed because release() at line 206 zeroes sl->id and invalidates the index), not depth recursion.

Refutations attempted and failed: (1) No alternative reading survives, since the comment's last sentence names the C stack at delegation depth as the reason; and the same file writes its other ancestry walk iteratively at src/dvs.c:943 ("for (walk = sl->parent; walk != 0; walk = dvs_parent(sw, walk))"), showing the promise was meant literally. (2) No earlier check bounds depth: src/dvs.c:168 "sw->nslots = (max_instances != 0) ? max_instances : DVS_DEFAULT_MAX;" caps nothing, src/dvs.h:99-104 documents no maximum, grep for "depth" in dvs.c hits only the comment, and spawns_per_step limits rate not total while 9.1.1 grants unlimited redelegation. (3) The code is reachable: dvs_kill (src/dvs.h:155), the guest lifecycle path do_kill at src/dvs.c:945 after its ancestry check, and the fault paths at src/dvs.c:1149 and 1201. (4) The doc does not state the limitation anywhere: all six "kill" hits in doc/Messaging.md were read, section 17 (line 2439 onward) has no correction on it, and 9.1.1 line 837 asserts the opposite ("Parentage is a single field per instance, depth is unbounded, and subtree kill means what it says"), while the M7 record at line 2170 calls the mechanism "subtree recursion".

Two caveats that lower confidence without sinking it: the cited location is stale (522-527/532 match HEAD^; at HEAD the comment is 569-572 and the call 578, and current line 532 is an unrelated "if (detail != NULL) {" in emit_event), and the crash scenario is overstated -- ~130k nested frames on a default 8 MB stack requires ~130k simultaneously live instances each with its own lua_State (build -> dv_new at src/dvs.c:354), several GB of RAM, and O(n^2) construction because find/claim are linear scans. The demonstrable defect is the one the reporter foregrounds: a false statement in the tree asserting a mitigation that is absent, on a function whose only real bound is a host-chosen, uncapped nslots. Tests reach three generations at most (test/dvs_check.c:483-497), so nothing covers depth.

*Correction to the report:* Location is wrong and must be restated: the comment is at src/dvs.c:569-572 and the recursive call at src/dvs.c:578 (function declared src/dvs.c:561). The reported 522-527/532 belong to commit HEAD^; src/dvs.c:532 in the current tree is "if (detail != NULL) {" inside emit_event, unrelated to kill.

Failure-scenario framing should be trimmed: dvs_new(&h, 300000, 0) is legal (src/dvs.c:168, no cap; src/dvs.h:99-104 documents no maximum), but overflowing an 8 MB stack needs ~130k nested frames, hence ~130k simultaneously live instances each holding a lua_State, several GB of RAM, and O(n^2) setup work since find/claim scan linearly. The count falls to roughly 8-16k on a host thread with a 512 KB-1 MB stack. So the crash is reachable in a plausible embedding but is not cheap; the finding stands on the false claim, not the crash.

Severity as filed (worth-recording) is right. The proposed fix is also right, and the second option is the cheaper one: state the real bound (recursion depth cannot exceed nslots, so it is safe only while nslots is small), since nothing in the doc or dvs.h currently tells a host raising max_instances that it is also raising a C-stack bound.
