### R30. A guest escapes its instruction budget through a child and hangs dvs_step forever

audit `hostile-guest`

The code quotes are accurate, but both prongs of the claim fail.

CODE VERIFIED. /home/user/diluvium/src/dvs.c:859 is `uint64_t insns = 0, mem = 0;`, :892-893 are `field_int(msg, len, "instructions", &insns); field_int(msg, len, "memory_kb", &mem);`, :362 is `if (req->instructions != 0 || req->memory_kb != 0) {`, and /home/user/diluvium/src/dv.c:571 is `if (inst->insn_limit != 0) {`. So yes: an absent `instructions` field yields insn_limit 0 and no count hook, and dv.c treats 0 as "no limit" by design (dv.c:191 `if (inst->insn_limit != 0 && inst->insn_used >= inst->insn_limit)`).

PRONG (b), the doc contradiction, is refuted by the surrounding section. doc/Messaging.md:918-919 is quoted correctly, but Part 5 states the opposite reading normatively and *earlier*, not later:
  - :813, inside 9.1's own worked spawn request, the *guest* writes the budget: `  budget = { instructions = 5e6, memory_kb = 512 },`
  - :848-849, 9.1.2 item 5: "Enforcing per-instance budgets (9.4). It enforces the numbers; it does not decide them."
  - :857-858: "Enforcing a budget is mechanism. Choosing what a child's budget should be, and what to do when it is exceeded, is a program."
  - src/dvs.h:17-24 repeats item 5 and "deciding what a child's budget should be is a program."
9.4's sentence is about the *mechanism* being non-cooperative and living below the guest, and the code implements exactly that: the hook is armed by the ABI, the numbers live on the slot, and no guest-reachable path can alter an instance's own limit (the lifecycle ops are spawn/kill/query/hibernate only — dvs.c:1085-1093 — and dv_set_budget refuses a started instance, dv.c). "A guest cannot meaningfully limit itself" is not violated by a parent choosing a child's number; that is item 5 verbatim. The reporter concedes this reading and then overrides it, which is not a contradiction in the tree.

PRONG (a), the hang, is not a defect of the code under review. dvs.c never calls dv_run. dvs_step's third loop calls `keep = sw->host.drive(sw->host.ud, id, sl->inst, sl->ctx);` (dvs.c:~1164) — a host callback. dvs.h:78-84 and doc 11.5 put everything below the vtable on the host, and 9.4's own table row is "| Wall clock | host side |" (doc:926), so the doc never promises this layer bounds a runaway child. The claim "the host has no call it can make" is also false in the prevention direction: build() calls `sw->host.create(sw->host.ud, sl->id, sl->inst)` after dv_load and before anything is ever driven (dvs.c:377-378), and dv_set_budget only refuses when `inst->started` — so a host that wants a ceiling can impose one on every child in `create`, which dvs.h:78 describes as receiving an instance "already made, loaded and budgeted".

No budget-attenuation rule exists to violate. 9.3 (doc:903) is "A supervisor must never grant a child more capability than it holds itself" — capability, which dvs.c:884-889 does enforce via dvs_may_grant. The M7 correction at doc:2288-2289 also scopes the trust boundary: "9 treats a guest as untrusted with respect to capability". Budgets are per-instance numbers (doc:152), never described as attenuating.

EVIDENCE UNREPRODUCIBLE. `test/dvs_attack3.c` does not exist in this checkout and `git status --short` is empty, so the quoted `[EXIT=124]` and `--budgeted-child` transcripts cannot be checked against anything in the tree. Independently, `grep -n instructions test/dvs_check.c` returns nothing: no test in the tree sets a child budget at all, so even the "control case proves the mechanism works" half rests on a file that isn't here.

SEPARATE OBSERVATION (not this finding, but demonstrable and checkable). do_spawn reads a *top-level* `instructions`/`memory_kb`, while diluvium_mp_field (src/dmsgpack.c:2101-2117) reads one map and iterates only its own keys, skipping values — it does not descend. doc/Messaging.md:813 is the only place the doc shows the field, and it shows it nested: `budget = { instructions = 5e6, memory_kb = 512 }`. So a guest that copies 9.1's example verbatim gets insns=0, mem=0 and an unbudgeted child, silently and with no event. That is a doc-shape-vs-code mismatch with a concrete input, and it is what a real finding in this area would have to be about; it is not the finding submitted.

---

### R31. 'query' has no descendant rule, so any lifecycle holder can enumerate the whole swarm and read each instance's usage

audit `hostile-guest`

The code quote is accurate and the mechanism is real: src/dvs.c:1021 is exactly `sl = find(sw, (dvs_id)target);`, find() (src/dvs.c:147-156) scans all slots with no parentage constraint, do_query (1011-1031) has no ancestry walk while do_kill (925-950) and do_hibernate (963-1008) both do, and do_query is reachable via drain at src/dvs.c:1093 gated only by dvs_holds(..., DVS_CAP_LIFECYCLE). So a lifecycle holder can enumerate ids and read each slot's alive/dead state and usage. But it fails the audit's reportability bar on all four counts I checked.

(1) No doc sentence is contradicted. The cited section 4.0 reads in full: "**There is exactly one isolation boundary in this system: the instance.** An instance has its own heap, its own queues, its own capability set, its own budget, and its own snapshot. Nothing else crosses it but msgpack bytes." (doc/Messaging.md:151-154), and its own explanation is about references: "Because upvalues and object references cannot leave a `lua_State`, there is no rule needed to prevent them crossing an instance boundary. It is structurally impossible rather than enforced." (161-163). A `status` event is msgpack bytes delivered through the documented system/events protocol -- the sanctioned channel, not a breach of it. "its own budget" is per-instance accounting, which section 9.4 assigns to the swarm layer ("The swarm layer owns per-instance limits"), not a confidentiality guarantee. The doc treats confidentiality as a separate named property and says explicitly when a check does not provide it -- 10.9, line 1386: "Section 10.6's capability check does not cover this: it gates *restore*, not *confidentiality*." It never claims confidentiality for the capability set anywhere (grepped for confidential/cannot observe/cannot read/leak/enumerate).

(2) The doc reports query's line exactly as built. Section 9.2's table (line 875) lists the lifecycle contents as "spawn, kill, query, hibernate" with no scope qualifier, and the "As built" text extends the ancestry rule to exactly two ops: "With an `id` it swaps out a descendant, on the same ancestry rule as `kill`" (891-893). Section 13's mutation-verified list names "the descendant rule on kill" (line 2171) -- kill alone. The code comments match (dvs.c:939 "Only an ancestor may kill", dvs.c:958 "on the same ancestry rule as kill"). Nothing states or implies query is scoped, so no statement in the doc is false and convention 3 is not violated.

(3) There is no wrong result and no crash. do_query answers correctly for the id asked. The reporter's own kind is "unchecked-property" and the argument is explicitly from silence -- "nothing in 9.2 says query is unrestricted" -- which is the excluded "this looks risky" shape. The proposed fix (add an ancestry rule, or mint a new attenuable capability name) is a design change, not a defect repair.

(4) The measured evidence is not at the cited path. test/dvs_attack2.c and test/dvs_attack3.c do not exist in /home/user/diluvium (test/*.c is dhash_check, dshim_check, dsnap_check, dtask_check, dv_check, dvs_check, mp_cursor_fuzz, snap_harness). They live in the reporter's own worktree at /home/user/diluvium/.claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-10/test/dvs_attack2.c:201-202. The behavior is still confirmable by reading src/dvs.c, so this does not falsify the mechanism, but the "Measured" block is not reproducible at the path cited.

Confidence is "likely" rather than "certain" because the kill/hibernate/query asymmetry is genuine and a reasonable reviewer could want it closed; what I am certain of is that it is not demonstrable as a wrong result, a crash, or a false statement in doc/Messaging.md, which is what this audit asks for.

---

## What each audit covered

Coverage notes in the auditors' own words, including the clauses they traced to a
test and found sound. Useful in the other direction: it says what has been looked
at, so a later pass can look somewhere else.

### audit `m0-m2`

Scope covered: doc/Messaging.md sections 5, 6 and 7.3/7.5 (reached via the codec), and the M0/M0b/M1/M2 milestone entries at doc/Messaging.md:1712-1791, clause by clause, against src/dmsgpack.c, src/dqueue.c, src/dtask.c, src/dendpoint.c, test/test_msgpack.lua, test/test_queue.lua, test/test_wait.lua, test/test_endpoint.lua and test/dtask_check.c.

Clauses I traced to a test that does assert them, and found sound: M1's integer-width cutoffs (both sides of all eight, as wire bytes, test/test_msgpack.lua:74-90); floats always float64; empty table as map and the whole 5.3 array/map rule (mp_is_array at src/dmsgpack.c:592 is correct — distinct keys plus max == count does establish density); forced shapes; the cycle message; the key path `a.b[1]`; the offset chain; every reserved ext code (I probed 0x00-0x10 directly and all refuse by name, including 0x08, which the test omits but the code at src/dmsgpack.c:1101 handles). M2's 6.4 rows, no-raise outcomes, copy-not-reference, ring wrap, pop-never-parks (a genuine coroutine test), stale handle, and the proc_10ms chain. M0's error/traceback propagation, result-count adjustment, caller stack, and the loud top-level yield.

Evidence method, given the no-make/no-gcc constraint: the tree is clean at dbe9732 and dist/diluvium_debug (16:41) postdates every src mtime, so I used it plus dist/dtask_check as read-only oracles. Every finding above was reproduced by running one of those binaries with `-e`, and the two memory measurements come from /proc/self/status VmHWM inside the interpreter. Beyond the individual clauses I ran two randomised differential tests that found nothing: 300 queues x 200 random push/pop steps across reject/drop_oldest/drop_newest against a Lua list model (0 status, value, order, length or capacity mismatches), and 4000 random nested values through encode/decode compared deep including math.type (0 mismatches). The queue ring and the codec's round-trip fidelity look genuinely right.

What I could not reach. I could not verify M0's mitigation-removal claims ("re-raise removed gives 3 failures, plain lua_pcall gives 5, dropped lua_checkstack aborts under api_check") or the stripped-size numbers, since both need a compiler. The check counts have drifted by one in each file (msgpack 140 vs the doc's 139, queue 288 vs 287) — too trivial to file. Two weak-but-not-filed assertions worth knowing about: test/test_queue.lua:202 matches the key path by searching the error for "bad", which "bad argument #2 to 'push'" would also satisfy, and test/dtask_check.c:161 crosses 64 results that are all nil, so only their count is checked, not their values. I did not audit M4-M7 except where the M1 codec reaches into them, and I did not run the sanitiser builds, the fuzzers, or interrupt_check.sh.

### audit `m3-m4`

Read in full: doc/Messaging.md sections 6.3-6.6, 7.5, 8.1-8.5, 11.1-11.6, milestones M0-M8 and section 17; src/dv.h, src/dv.c, src/dtask.c, src/dqueue.h, all host-facing and wait/push/pop paths of src/dqueue.c, src/dendpoint.c's ABI-facing half, src/lua.c's task-mode code; test/dv_check.c, test/dtask_check.c, test/test_wait.lua, test/run_tests.sh, the relevant parts of test/test_queue.lua; bindings/python/src/diluvium/__init__.py, bindings/js/src/index.js and its integration test, bindings/rust/diluvium/src/lib.rs push/peek/waitset paths, bindings/rust/diluvium-wasmtime/src/lib.rs status mapping, .github/workflows/test.yml's js-binding job.

ANSWER TO THE UNTESTED-ABI QUESTION: none. All 27 functions declared in src/dv.h — dv_abi_version, dv_status_name, dv_new, dv_free, dv_load, dv_last_error, dv_queue_lookup, dv_queue_state, dv_queue_push, dv_queue_pop, dv_queue_peek, dv_queue_release, dv_run, dv_resume, dv_waitset_get, dv_set_endpoint_handler, dv_endpoint_allow, dv_endpoint_queue, dv_endpoint_close, dv_set_budget, dv_usage, dv_exceeded, dv_snapshot, dv_restore, dv_register_code, dv_layout, dv_set_notify — are called from at least one test under test/. Every one is called from test/dv_check.c; dv_waitset_get, dv_snapshot and dv_restore are additionally driven by test/snap_harness.c and test/dvs_check.c. I grepped all of test/, bindings/ (excluding bindings/rust/target) and script/ per name to confirm, so the "untested ABI function" list is empty. Three narrower gaps in that surface, offered as fact rather than as findings: the un-set paths `dv_set_notify(inst, NULL, ud)` (dv.c:414) and `dv_set_endpoint_handler(inst, NULL, ud)` (dv.c:440) are never exercised — every call site passes a real callback and NULL only as `ud`; no test passes NULL as `inst` to anything, which is how finding 2's dead guard survived; and `DV_ABI_MISMATCH` is declared (dv.h:65) and named (dv.c:77) but returned by no function, since dv_new signals a version mismatch by returning NULL.

Verified-honest claims worth recording as non-findings: test_wait.lua contains exactly 58 checks on the executed path, matching doc:1799; bindings/python has 17 tests, bindings/js 15 codec plus 11 WASI, all matching doc:2176-2177; test/dv_check.c includes only dv.h (plus stdio/string/stdlib), so doc:1836's "written against dv.h alone" holds; the pcall/xpcall yield assertions (test_wait.lua:117-139) and the VM-dispatched-metamethod complement (188-202) are real and mitigation-sensitive; the readiness-beats-closed ordering (dqueue.c:419-431) fails visibly if the two loops are swapped, asserted at test_wait.lua:66; the deadlock cases are genuinely run in subprocesses (test_wait.lua:284-306).

Could not reach: I did not build or run anything (hard constraint), so every mitigation-removal claim above is argued from the code path rather than observed red, and all size-budget figures in M3/M4 are unverified. CI outcomes are external — M4b's "the integration test has now passed" cannot be checked here; I confirmed only that .github/workflows/test.yml:296-298 does invoke bindings/js/test/instance.integration.mjs against dist/diluvium_wasi.wasm. I did not audit the generator for bindings/js/test/vectors.json: msgpack.test.js:3 points at "the generator in the commit that added this" rather than a file in the tree, so the claim at doc:2192 that the vectors were produced by src/dmsgpack.c is not reproducible from the working tree and would drift silently if the C encoder changed — I left it out of the findings because I could not demonstrate a divergence. Rust test counts (14 in diluvium/tests/host.rs plus 1 in diluvium-sys) fall one short of doc:1836's "16 Rust tests"; too weak to report. M5, M6 and M7 were out of scope and I only read them where dv.c's snapshot and endpoint entry points required it.

### audit `m5`

SCOPE: read doc/Messaging.md sections 4.2, 6.1-6.6, 7.1-7.5, 8.1-8.5, 9.3, 9.5, 10.8 and the M5 milestone record (lines 1861-1903); src/dendpoint.{c,h} in full; src/dqueue.c push/declare/destroy/info and all host-facing endpoint entry points; src/dv.{c,h} endpoint section; src/dmsgpack.c ext 0x02 decode and snapshot-mode table encoding; src/dsnap.c permanents; src/dlibs.c; test/test_endpoint.lua, test/test_queue.lua, test/test_msgpack.lua, test/dv_check.c, test/dvs_check.c; bindings/rust/diluvium-wasmtime (lib.rs + sandboxed.rs) and bindings/js msgpack tests. Claims about Lua internals were checked in THIS tree (lua_tolstring at src/lapi.c:415-434). No make/gcc/writes; the four behavioural demonstrations were run with the already-built dist/diluvium_linux_x86_64 using -e only, which writes nothing.

THE DELIVERY TABLE, ROW BY ROW. There is no single table titled "delivery" in section 8; the delivery outcomes are spread over three places, and I enumerated all of them. Section 8 itself (8.1-8.5) contains no delivery table — it is yield-aware hostcalls, coroutine hosting, the wait-set, non-yieldable contexts and permission checks.

(A) 7.2's outcome set, as it applies to an endpoint. Endpoints are declared at dendpoint.c:241 with capacity 0 (-> the 64 default) and on_full hardcoded to DQ_REJECT, and endpoint.bind takes no opts, so an endpoint's policy is not guest-selectable.
  1. live endpoint with space -> ACCEPTED "ok". TESTED: dv_check.c:474 (guest sees "ok"), dv_check.c:478-480 (host drains the bytes).
  2. far end closed -> "gone", immediately, no raise, guest side. TESTED: dv_check.c:490-491.
  3. far end closed -> DV_QUEUE_GONE, host side. TESTED: dv_check.c:494-495.
  4. endpoint at capacity -> "full" (reject). NOT TESTED on an endpoint anywhere. "full" is covered only on ordinary queues (test_queue.lua:97-98, 154, 216).
  5. endpoint disabled -> "disabled". NOT TESTED. Reachable: queue.disable has no endpoint guard (dqueue.c:790-799), so a guest can disable its own endpoint and its pushes then answer "disabled" rather than "gone".
  6. push to a destroyed endpoint handle -> RAISES per 6.2. NOT TESTED on an endpoint.
  7. BLOCKS: 7.2 says push "may yield on local capacity when on_full = \"block\"" — unreachable through endpoint.bind, since the policy is hardcoded reject. So "a push to an endpoint never blocks" holds structurally, and NO TEST asserts it; nothing would fail if dendpoint.c:241 were changed to DQ_BLOCK. Test-plan row 14 ("Push to full, disabled, and gone destinations. Assert none of them raise and none of them block", doc:2290-2291) is satisfied for ordinary queues and, for endpoints, only for the "gone" third.
  8. "dropped_oldest" on an endpoint -> unreachable (reject). "empty" popping an empty endpoint -> covered generically, not on an endpoint. "timeout"/"closed" are queue.wait statuses (test_wait.lua), not endpoint-specific.
(B) endpoint.status.
  9. fresh -> "live". TESTED dv_check.c:472 and again on the pre-auth path dv_check.c:568.
  10. after close -> "gone". TESTED dv_check.c:488.
  11. once gone stays gone. NOT TESTED. dendpoint.c:109-111 only acts when live==0 and dv.h exposes no re-open call, so the property is structural; nothing removes the guard and observes a failure.
  12. status on a non-endpoint -> raises. TESTED test_endpoint.lua:65-67. On an unissued handle, is_endpoint false: test_endpoint.lua:68.
  13. status on a destroyed endpoint -> raises "is not an endpoint" (misleading wording, no wrong value). NOT TESTED.
(C) endpoint.bind.
  14. host refuses -> raises "the host refused this reference". TESTED dv_check.c:517-524.
  15. no callback and no pre-authorised bytes -> raises "binds no endpoints". TESTED test_endpoint.lua:54-57.
  16. non-reference argument -> raises. TESTED test_endpoint.lua:43-48 (five shapes) — but see finding 1: the check it exercises is bypassable.
  17. same token twice -> same handle (dendpoint.c:232-239). NOT TESTED, and finding 3 is a wrong result on that path.
  18. name already declared -> raises "'%s' is already declared" (dendpoint.c:243). NOT TESTED.
  19. relay across three instances with no runtime routing (7.4) -> TESTED dv_check.c:573-666, and the hop count really does prove each message transited the relay.
  20. ext 0x02 with resolver -> reference. TESTED test_msgpack.lua:237-240, test_endpoint.lua:31-35. Without a resolver -> opaque ext. TESTED in bindings/js/test/msgpack.test.js:118-120, exactly as doc:1876-1878 claims. Both halves of the 4.2 seam check out.
(D) 9.5's failure-policy rows.
  21. push to a dead instance -> "gone". TESTED dvs_check.c:795.
  22. non-resident WITH wake_on_message -> accepted into a bounded host buffer, "ok", drained ahead of live pushes. TESTED dvs_check.c:685-709, plus the bound at dvs_check.c:757-788. The M5 record honestly lists this as "Not done" at M5 (doc:1902-1903) and M7 delivers it.
  23. non-resident WITHOUT wake_on_message -> "gone". TESTED dvs_check.c:714-751.

dv_endpoint_allow: THE PRE-AUTHORISATION PATH IS TESTED, not only the callback. test/dv_check.c:544-569 (endpoint_preauthorised) installs no handler at all — "No handler installed at all -- only a pre-authorised reference" — pre-authorises "9"->42, and asserts the bind succeeds, that dv_endpoint_queue(42) is non-zero, and that status reads "live". The wasmtime wrapper exercises the same path (bindings/rust/diluvium-wasmtime/src/lib.rs:574-586, examples/sandboxed.rs:82). What is NOT tested is the composition dv.h:290-291 promises ("Registered references are consulted before any handler set by dv_set_endpoint_handler, so the two compose"): no test installs both, so nothing would fail if the order at dendpoint.c:214-230 were inverted. Reading the code, the order is correct. I did not report that as a finding — it is a coverage gap, not a defect.

CONVENTION 2 (mutation verification). The doc records mutation verification for M2, M3, M6/dshim and dvs_check, and makes no such claim for M5, so there is no false claim here — but M5's endpoint tests have not been mutation-verified, and one of them demonstrably passes for the wrong reason: test/test_endpoint.lua:34 asserts "there is nothing inside a reference a program should read" by probing ref.anything, a key never set, while ref[1] returns the payload (finding 1).

SECTION 8.5. No capability check point exists anywhere in src/ — grep for "permission", "capability check" and the (capability, resource, context) signature finds nothing outside licence text. This is NOT a finding: 8.5 says "The token model itself is separate work", doc:46 says "the per-capability permission points are separate work", and no milestone claims 8.5 is implemented. Worth recording for whoever lands it: endpoint.bind is the call that turns an opaque host reference into write access to a far end, and dendpoint.c has no marked location where the check would go.

THINGS I EXAMINED AND DELIBERATELY DID NOT REPORT: endpoint.bind silently ignoring the name argument when a token is already bound (returns the first handle, so queue.lookup(second_name) is nil) — deliberate per dendpoint.c:232-234 and no wrong value results; endpoint.status/is_endpoint saying "is not an endpoint" about a destroyed endpoint (wording only); queue.info returning a seventh field `endpoint` that doc:427-428's enumeration omits (additive, and test_endpoint.lua:64 depends on it); the misplaced doc comment in dv.h:270-278, which describes dv_endpoint_queue but sits above dv_endpoint_allow's block, leaving dv.h:296 uncommented; dvs_check.c:685 citing "8.4's row" for what is 9.5's row. GAP I COULD NOT CLOSE: findings 3, 5 and 6 are derived from code reading plus the confirmed behaviour of the primitives they call, not from an executed end-to-end demonstration — each needs a host that resolves references, and building test/dv_check.c was out of bounds. Findings 1, 2 and 4's preconditions were each executed against the prebuilt binary and the outputs are quoted verbatim.

### audit `m6`

Scope: M6's seven acceptance clauses (doc/Messaging.md:1911-1915) and §10.7's six preconditions (lines 1331-1377), read against src/dshim.c, src/dsnap.c (thread capture/restore), src/dqueue.c, src/dtask.c, src/dv.c, and the core files the shim depends on (ldo.c, lapi.c, lfunc.c, lstate.h). Tests read in full: test/dshim_check.c, test/dsnap_check.c (thread, closure and header sections), test/snap_harness.c, test/fingerprint_check.sh, script/fuzz_snapshot.py. Nothing was compiled or run (hard constraint), so all three findings are demonstrated by tracing code and quoting it, not by observing a failure.

CLAUSE BY CLAUSE.

(1) "an agent parked on `queue.wait` round-trips and resumes" -- ESTABLISHED, in-process. test/dsnap_check.c:2021 parks through the real driver (`diluvium_task_pushbody`), asserts LUA_YIELD after the trip, `okf(withk >= 2, "with both of its C continuations restored", withk, 2)`, pushes the message by name via `diluvium_queue_find`/`diluvium_queue_push_bytes`, fires with `diluvium_queue_fire`, and asserts `strcmp(got, "woke:hello") == 0`. Narrower than the clause in one respect: `protected_roundtrip` (line 126) encodes and decodes in the *same* lua_State, so permanents, continuation names and the queue subsystem are trivially present. Cross-process restore is covered separately by test/snap_harness.c (--emit / --load in different processes), which the doc credits with catching the unregistered driver continuation.

(2) "shared upvalues remain shared" -- ESTABLISHED. two_closures_still_share_an_upvalue (1147) mutates through `inc` three times and reads 3 through `get`; an_unshared_upvalue_stays_unshared (1176) is the converse; an_open_upvalue_stays_open (1903) resumes the coroutine so it writes 99 to the local and then asks the closure, which is the only assertion that distinguishes an alias from a copied value. The mutation record ("Emitting UPVAL where UPJOIN belongs turns two_closures_still_share_an_upvalue red -- and nothing else") satisfies convention 2 for this clause.

(3) "a mismatched runtime identity refuses cleanly" -- ESTABLISHED, but split across two narrower halves that never meet. a_foreign_runtime_is_refused (887) fabricates an all-zeros `runtime` field and asserts DILUVIUM_SNAP_RUNTIME_MISMATCH; the_runtime_check_comes_first (932) fixes the diagnostic order. fingerprint_check.sh proves the *premise* -- two real builds fingerprint differently while `LUA_RELEASE|LUAC_VERSION|LUAC_FORMAT` are identical -- but it only prints fingerprints; no test takes a real snapshot under one build and feeds it to the other. Also note fingerprint_check.sh:63 (`[ -f .data/onelua.c ] || cp -r src/* .data`) reuses a pre-existing .data, so it can measure a stale tree.

(4) "a hibernate attempted below a continuation-less C frame refuses with a named error" -- NARROWER, and the doc says so elsewhere. The shape is unreachable: yield_across_plain_pcall_never_parks (test/dshim_check.c:663) shows the thread unwinds instead of parking, and §10.2:1022-1030 plus §17:2420 record that "there is no reachable suspended thread with a continuation-less C frame below its yield." So the clause is met only at the predicate level, by the_exemption_is_narrow (891), which fills in a `diluvium_frame` by hand: `okf(diluvium_shim_framecapturable(&f, 0) == DILUVIUM_SNAP_C_FRAME, "a continuation-less C frame below the yield is refused", ...)`. The named error at the layer a host actually calls (src/dsnap.c:1346, "snapshot: this thread cannot be captured: %s") is never exercised for this code, and the restore-side twin DILUVIUM_RES_NO_CONTINUATION (src/dshim.c:455) has no test at all. That is honest in §10.2 but the flat "All met." at line 1915 does not carry the qualification.

(5) "a snapshot taken with a live `defer` in scope round-trips" -- ESTABLISHED, and it is the strongest thread test in the file. a_pending_close_round_trips (1946) checks the original has a tbclist entry, then the copy does, then resets the CLOSED global before resuming so the deferred block's effect cannot be inherited from the original, then asserts both "done" and CLOSED. Because `defer` desugars to a self-referential `setmetatable(t, t)`, a wrong slot or a missing metatable fixup would leave __close uncalled and CLOSED false.

(6) "a foreign host stamp is refused" -- ESTABLISHED. the_host_stamp_is_asymmetric (965) asserts all four directions, including the two that make stamping non-advisory (stamped snapshot refused where no stamp is expected; unstamped refused by a stamping host) and a different host name refused. Header-layer only, but that is the layer `diluvium_snap_load` calls.

(7) "the fuzzer reports no crashes" -- LITERALLY MET, but it is evidence about the payload digest and not about the frame validator. `dv_snapshot` writes a header carrying a SHA-256 of the payload (src/dsnap.c:366-373) and `checkheader` verifies it (src/dsnap.c:508-524). Every mutant script/fuzz_snapshot.py produces is a change *inside the payload* (`find_thread_record` anchors on the ext 0x08 record) and the fuzzer never recomputes the digest, so all 430 are refused at the header before `diluvium_shim_checkframes` is reached. The docstring's own measurement says as much ("With the digest as well: 0"), and §17:2508-2510 states the two-layer split, so nothing here is a false claim -- but per convention 2 the current green run could not go red if the frame checks regressed, and the doc's test inventory entry titled "Snapshot validation" (line 2340) reads as though it covers them. A digest recomputed after mutation would turn this into the test the title claims.

§10.7'S SIX PRECONDITIONS, WITH THEIR CODE.
1. No continuation-less C frame below the call site -- src/dshim.c:295 (`if (!is_innermost && f->is_c && !f->has_k) return DILUVIUM_SNAP_C_FRAME;`), reached via diluvium_shim_capturable (dshim.c:318) from src/dsnap.c:1344; mirrored on restore at dshim.c:454.
2. No live host resource / userdata without `__persist` -- not in dshim.c; src/dsnap.c:1023-1048 refuses every full userdata by name ("release its userdata first (10.7 item 2)"). Blanket refusal rather than a hook, which the doc records as the not-done item.
3. No light userdata the runtime has not named -- not in dshim.c; src/dmsgpack.c:880-897, with the permanents lookup deliberately first (dmsgpack.c:862-875) so `dqueue.parkmark` (src/dsnap.c:255) resolves before the refusal.
4. Single thread -- NO CODE ANYWHERE. Finding 3.
5. The thread must actually be suspended -- src/dshim.c:309-310, status plus frame count, which is what separates running/`normal` from fresh.
6. Not inside a debug hook -- src/dshim.c:311-312 (`allowhook` shortcut) and the frame walk at dshim.c:86 + 282-283 testing CIST_HOOKED|CIST_HOOKYIELD, which is the one that actually fires (test/dshim_check.c:744).

NOT REPORTED, deliberately. (a) `old_errfunc` is also dropped from the wire format, so a restored dv instance loses `dv_msghandler` (L->errfunc is a lua_State field and is never restored) -- guest errors after a restore come back without a traceback. §10.2:999 names restoring the error-handler chain as out of scope, so this is a documented gap; the funcidx half is not, which is why only that is filed. (b) `f.ctx` is serialized as 32 bits (src/dsnap.c:1393) while `lua_KContext` is `ptrdiff_t`; harmless for every continuation in the tree (dq_wait uses 0, dq_push a small queue id, finishpcall 0 or 2), but a host continuation that stores a pointer in ctx would be truncated. No such host exists here, so it is not demonstrable today. (c) test/dshim_check.c:522 `resumer_thread_is_refused` asserts acceptance, not refusal -- a naming mismatch only; the real mid-resume case is the separate function below it.

### audit `m7`

Read in full: src/dvs.h, src/dvs.c, test/dvs_check.c, doc/Messaging.md sections 9.1-9.5, 10.6, 10.7, 10.10, 11.5, 13's M6/M7/M8 records and all of 17. Every claim about a dv_/queue/codec call was checked against this tree: src/dv.c (dv_last_error/set_error ownership, dv_usage, dv_exceeded, dv_snapshot's size enquiry, dv_restore, dv_run's lua_sethook, dv_queue_push), src/dqueue.c (push_bytes on_full policies, dq_take's decode), src/dmsgpack.c (diluvium_mp_field is a top-level-keys-only scan; mp_need raises on truncation; decode ignores trailing bytes), src/llex.c (error tokens are not truncated), src/lundump.h (LUAC_FORMAT).

Both directions of 9.1.2 checked. Items 1-4 are implemented. Item 5 is implemented for dvs_root but not for the documented spawn request (finding 4) and evaporates after a wake (finding 1); 9.4's "total queued messages" row has no mechanism in this layer at all, which I did not report because I could not construct a failure from it -- the row's Mechanism column describes computability, not a limit the layer sets. Item 6 is implemented except 10.10's stamp (finding 7). I found no layering violation in the other direction: dvs.c includes only dv.h and dmsgpack.h, needs no lua_State, and the two things not literally in the list -- emit_event into system/events and the `query` op -- reduce to items 4 and 1 and are sanctioned by 9.2's own table. 9.2's reserved-queue contents (spawn, kill, query, hibernate) are all handled by drain. 9.5's five rows all hold, subject to findings 8 (a drop_oldest push is accepted but reported DVS_LIMIT) and the ordering claim, which I verified is structural: the pending drain is inside dvs_wake before it returns.

Not reached, because I was forbidden to build or run: nothing was executed, so no mutation verification of my own and no ASan confirmation of finding 2 -- the argument there is from source (dv_free frees inst->error at dv.c:276) rather than from a sanitizer report. The doc's "66 checks" and "11.0 KB" figures for M7 are unverified; test/dvs_check.c contains 100 static ok/okf call sites, but branch structure makes a static count meaningless, so I am not claiming a discrepancy. I did not audit dsnap.c or the restore validator itself (M6 territory), only the calls dvs.c makes into them. I did not attempt to verify whether a guest can obtain string.dump in this fork, so the bytecode half of finding 9 is argued from the host-injected path instead.

### audit `single-point`

Read in full: .github/workflows/{build,test,release,fix-release-flags}.yml, Makefile, test/run_tests.sh, test/fingerprint_check.sh, test/interrupt_check.sh, script/wasi_check.sh, script/patch_series.sh, script/fuzz_snapshot.py, script/fuzz_exec.py, script/fuzz_struct.py, doc/Messaging.md sections 4.1/4.2/14/15/16/17 and the M4b-M8 milestone record. Cross-checked every claim against the tree: main.lua:82/201 and src/lua.h:519 (skip reason accurate), api.lua:4-7 (self-skip accurate), files.lua _port gates at 86/726/777/866/902, literals.lua:299 locale gate, dmsgpack.h:22 / dvs.c:26 include chain, .data contents, fork-point reachability, and the for-loop errexit semantics behind the patch_series vacuity (reproduced in a scratch shell).

Cleared as NOT findings after checking: `continue-on-error: ${{ matrix.experimental }}` in test.yml (both entries false, comment accurate); Makefile:392 `test_libs` best-effort build (backed by guard_no_test_libs, which loads a .so rather than stat-ing it); build.yml:339 build_stats `|| true` and :334 `gh release download` (summary-only, documented); release.yml:143 tag/VERSION `::warning::` (documented as advisory with a stated reason); fuzz_snapshot.py (has explicit anti-vacuity guards at :92 and :207) and fuzz_struct.py (:151 anchor guard; mutations() cannot yield zero for a located code array); the sanitize_checks and sanitizers-job loops (`set -e` plus non-empty globs); `nm -u` symbol check at Makefile:415 (`|| true` is bounded by the `-n "$leaked"` test); fingerprint_check.sh hardcoding -DLUA_USE_LINUX (compiles on Darwin under -DMAKE_LIB, no -Wl,-E, so no macOS break); `make build_platform || make` in the fuzz job (masks the error, but the next step dies loudly on an empty --bin, so not silently green); doc claims "47 tests run now" (--list returns exactly 47) and the fuzz/mutant counts.

Could not reach, so unverified: nothing was compiled or executed beyond read-only shell (no make/gcc per the hard constraint), so I could not empirically confirm that dvs.c compiles with a deliberate lstate.h include, nor that fuzz counts reproduce. I did not inspect bindings/{rust,python,js} test contents (a recursive grep there timed out and I did not retry), so the container-free companion tests the doc credits at lines 2570-2579 are taken as described rather than read. I have no access to CI run history, so I cannot say which jobs are currently green — every finding is derived from the workflow/Makefile/script text and the tree. Note the checkout is shared: commit bbb4509 landed mid-audit (doc/Messaging.md grew 2618 -> 2657 lines); it touched no CI, Makefile or run_tests.sh, and all cited line numbers were re-verified with grep/awk against the post-bbb4509 tree.

### audit `doc-claims`

Read doc/Messaging.md end to end and checked it against the tree with Read/Grep and read-only shell. No make, no gcc, no writes; I did run the already-built binaries in dist/ (diluvium_debug, dtask/dhash/dshim/dsnap/dv/dvs_check) read-only, which is how the check counts and the forgery/reference-encoding demonstrations were obtained. Note the tree moved under me mid-audit (commit 55966a6 landed and src/dvs.c grew at 17:42), so every line number cited above was re-verified afterwards.\n\nVerified as holding (not reported): lapi.c:1095 and lapi.c:1112 are exactly the lines 8.2.1 items 1-2 describe; ldebug.c:167 is inside lua_getstack's downward walk; the four conformance citations are exact (coroutine.lua:10-14, coroutine.lua:159-163, errors.lua:399, locals.lua:1177); CIST_C is 1u<<15 (lstate.h:233); luai_makeseed is a constant (luaconf.h:716) making the lauxlib.c:1150 fallback dead; ltests.h:146 sets MAXINDEXRK 1; LUAI_MAXSTACK lives in ldo.c:192; exactly 16 core files differ from fork point 7579fc9 and all 16 are on patch_series.sh's allowlist; depth caps 16/150 with LUAI_MAXCCALLS 200; DILUVIUM_WAIT_MAX 32; the whole 5.5 ext registry behaviour including 0x01's decQuad message, 0x00/0x09-0x0F 'reserved' errors and 0x03-0x08 rejected outside a snapshot; inbox/outbox auto-declared exported with capacity 64/reject/both; handles never reused; wait accepts a destroyed handle ('closed') and raises for one never issued; declare refuses a non-string name; every 6.4 status including drop_newest reporting 'full' and nil-vs-empty; deadlock reported and exit 1; --task/-i refused; isyieldable false by default and true under --task; dv.h's status enum incl. DV_QUEUE_DROPPED, the peek contract's 'do not call dv_run or dv_resume', dv_waitset_get, and dv_resume no longer synthesising a timeout (dv.c:632-651); dvs_host matches 11.5's block byte for byte; dvs_spawn is now static (dvs.c:61); dvs_budget/dvs_caps exist; the eight 9.2 event names; the rate limit deferring before consuming (dvs.c:1071-1074); wake_on_message overridable at hibernate; the wake buffer draining inside dvs_wake; permanents on tables before interning, the pcall/xpcall continuation canaries, the sorted fingerprint; fuzz_snapshot's 430-mutant record and mp_cursor_fuzz's 200000+200000 inputs; run_tests.sh's 47 run entries and two evaluated guards; wasmtime 43 + wasm_exceptions + gc-null; changelog.py now type-checking SCALARS; bindings (rmp-serde to_vec_named, Send-not-Sync, abi_version checked in all three, python 17 / js 15 / wasi 11 tests).\n\nCould not reach: the size numbers in 3.2 (no compiling) — attributions check out per file, but dtask.c (1.7 KB per M3/M4) and dlibs.c appear in no row, and the counted rows sum to 62.4 KB, i.e. 6.1% of 1 MiB against the paragraph's 'roughly 8%' (7.2% if the explicitly-uncounted swarm row is included, so I judged that inside 'roughly' and did not report it); musl and wasm measurements, which the doc itself flags as not done; every 'verified by mutation' claim, since removing a mitigation and re-running needs a build; CI job status and the JS wasm integration test (needs a container); and the Rust count '16 tests' — I find 14 in diluvium/tests/host.rs plus 1 in diluvium-sys, which is close enough that I did not report it.

### audit `wrong-reason`

SCOPE. The worktree was checked out at main (62e2ff1), where none of the messaging work exists; the branch under audit is claude/diluvium-messaging-supervision-ba6zku (4edaadc, \"dv_resume: stop inventing a timeout that never elapsed\"), which I reset my private worktree to. All results below are from 4edaadc.

BASELINES (all green): dv_check 150/0, dvs_check 81/0, dsnap_check 142/0, dshim_check 87/0, dtask_check 25/0, dhash_check 252/0, mp_cursor_fuzz 9/0, snap_fuzz 429 mutants/409 refused/20 accepted/0 crashed, test_ci passed 47 failed 0 skipped 3, sanitize_checks clean under asan+ubsan.

CHECK-COUNT vs ok() SITES. Rather than hand-counting (loops and several counter-incrementing helpers make raw grep counts meaningless), I built each test with --coverage and diffed executed against unexecuted assertion call sites. No mismatch of the kind asked about: every unexecuted site in dv_check, dvs_check, dsnap_check, dshim_check and dtask_check is an `ok(0, ...)` setup guard on a path not taken (197 unexecuted lines in dsnap_check, zero of which are non-guard assertions). The one `ok(1, ...)` pair worth checking, dv_check.c:1189/1192 (both arms of the memory-budget if/else assert unconditionally), is backed by a real assertion at 1194 -- disabling memory-budget enforcement in dv_alloc turns it red: \"[FAIL] and its peak stays inside the budget it was given (peak 1450194 KB against a 512 KB budget)\". Not a finding.

MUTATIONS RUN (~50 total, each applied alone, tree restored via git checkout of the single file between runs; a control mutation confirmed every target actually recompiles the mutated source). Verified CAUGHT, i.e. these tests are real evidence: the two newest commits' own claims reproduce exactly (re-opening the endpoint forgery hole at dmsgpack.c:1094 turns 3 dv_check checks red; restoring the synthesised timeout at dv.c:647 turns 3 red, including \"got DV_DONE, wanted DV_IDLE\"); 9.3 attenuation (dvs_may_grant and the trailing-* rule in implies()); the lifecycle capability gate on drain; the 9.5 rate limit's check-before-consume in drain; kill's ancestry rule; queue rules 6.2 destroyed-handle-raises, 6.2 wait rejects a never-issued handle, 6.3 capacity>0, 6.3 wait handle-count cap, 6.6 declare-refuses-existing-name, 6.4 host push to full and to gone; memory-budget enforcement.

I also probed the whole documented queue contract (§6.2/6.3/6.4/6.6) directly against the built runtime -- string-only names, re-declare refused, handles never reused, wait accepting a destroyed handle with \"closed\" while raising for a never-issued one, disabled queues still draining, drop_newest reporting \"full\" and keeping the oldest, drop_oldest reporting \"dropped_oldest\", \"empty\" vs a pushed nil, queue.info round-tripping exported/direction, inbox/outbox auto-declared exported -- and every claim held. I audited diluvium_mp_skip's ILP32 overflow fix by hand: the invariant pending <= c->left is maintained and every bound is checked by subtraction before any addition, so the overflow is unreachable at any width as §17 claims (I could not link a real -m32 binary; no 32-bit libc here).

GUARDS THAT ARE CORRECT BUT UNTESTED (deliberately not filed as findings, since the code is right and the bar excludes \"consider adding a test\" -- recorded so they are not mistaken for covered): do_hibernate's ancestry rule at dvs.c (kill's equivalent IS tested; the doc says hibernate uses \"the same ancestry rule as kill\"); field_caps' DVS_MAX_CAPS count bound, whose absence is a stack overflow of do_spawn's caps[][] from guest-controlled t.len; field_caps' non-array shape refusal; drain's oversized-request refusal; do_spawn's rate-limit backstop (its own comment says it is unreachable by design); and DV_QUEUE_DISABLED from dv_queue_push -- documented at src/dv.h:143-145 and mapped in all three bindings, but removing `return DILUVIUM_Q_DISABLED;` from diluvium_queue_push_bytes (dqueue.c:1032, the host path) is caught by nothing in test_ci or dv_check, while the guest-path equivalent is covered. §14's test-plan bullet \"Push to full, disabled, and gone destinations\" is satisfied for the guest path and for host full/gone, not for host disabled.

NOT REACHED. I did not run the wasm/WASI targets, verify_wasm, build_swarm_lib's symbol check, or the Rust/JS/Python binding test suites (no container with the pinned wasi-sdk, and I did not exercise cargo/npm/pytest); §17 notes those jobs were red for weeks, so they deserve independent attention. I did not audit dmsgpack.c's encoder, dhash.c beyond running its 252 vectors, the snapshot permanents/content-addressed-Proto machinery in depth, dlibs/drepl/dline, or the M8 packaging claims. Within the snapshot layer I concentrated on the restore-side validator; the capture-side preconditions (§10.7, diluvium_shim_framecapturable) are genuinely unit-tested in dshim_check and I found no problem there. One artifact is left in the worktree: test/forge_check.c, the end-to-end endpoint-forgery exploit for finding 2 (not wired into the Makefile). Both src/ mutations and all others were reverted; git status shows only that untracked file, and the baselines above re-verify clean.

### audit `new-c`

Worktree moved to 55966a6 (branch claude/diluvium-messaging-supervision-ba6zku) so src/dvs.c, src/dsnap.c, src/dqueue.c and doc/Messaging.md matched the main checkout byte for byte (diff -q, exit 0). Baseline confirmed: dvs_check 92/0, dsnap_check 142/0, dshim_check 87/0, dv_check 152/0, snap_fuzz 429 mutants 0 crashes. src/dvs.c and src/dshim.c were mutated during the audit and are restored - `git diff --stat` is empty; the only leftovers are the untracked probes in /home/user/diluvium/.claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-9/test/ (audit_probe.c, audit_afail.c, audit_chain.c, audit_reentry.c, audit_nul.c, audit_budget.c, afail.h, afail_env.c, afail_sweep.sh).

1. Allocation failure - covered, and clean. dvs.c has exactly five allocation sites (grep: dvs.c:164, 171, 322, 636, 756). I compiled dvs.c alone with `-include test/afail.h`, which redirects malloc/calloc to counting wrappers (nm confirms dvs.o references only af_malloc/af_calloc), linked it against the unmodified dvs_check.c and onelua.c under ASan+UBSan with detect_leaks=1, and failed the Nth allocation for every N in 1..86 (the whole run's total). Every N: no sanitizer output, no leak, no crash - only the expected [FAIL] lines from the injected failure. setcaps' partial failure, including the third of five, is correct: `sl->ncaps = i + 1` is advanced only after a successful copy, so release() frees exactly the pointers that exist. Targeted probes for dvs_root with five caps, do_spawn with three, dvs_hibernate's snapshot buffer and dvs_push's wake buffer all behave (DVS_ERROR / "capabilities refused" / instance stays resident / DVS_ERROR) with no half-built slot. This part of the brief produced nothing, and I am reporting that rather than padding it.

2. Bounds - checked by reading, no finding. DVS_MAX_CAPS: setcaps refuses > 32 and caps[] has 32 slots; field_caps refuses t.len > 32 into a 32-row store. DVS_MAX_CAP_LEN: n >= 96 refused, and field_caps uses e.len >= 96. DVS_MAX_QNAME: nlen >= 64 refused, so memcpy of nlen+1 <= 64 fits char[64]. DVS_MAX_PENDING checked before indexing. drain's `buf[8192]` uses `len > sizeof(buf)`, and field_str's `t.len >= cap` keeps code[8192] terminated. emit_event's buf[512] worst case is 226 bytes (map header + "event" + longest event name "hibernated" + "id" + uint32 + "detail" + a 191-byte clamped detail), so the map's pair count always matches what was written.

3. Re-entrancy - exercised, no finding. test/audit_reentry.c makes one drain call kill two children's subtrees, spawn two replacements that reclaim the freed slots, hibernate a third child with wake_on_message, query it and then kill it, while the driver pushes into the hibernated instance every step; under ASan+UBSan with leak detection the run is clean and the final instance count is consistent. drain's post-op re-check and dvs_step's `find(sw, id)` after host.drive do hold - I could not construct a stale pointer or a skipped slot, because no reachable op frees the slot whose drain is in progress (do_kill and do_hibernate refuse anything that is not a strict descendant).

4. Not reached, and worth someone's attention rather than a finding. ds_decodethread (src/dsnap.c:1467-1477) bounds nslots, nframes and capacity but never bounds ntbc, and never includes ntbc in its length check; ds_buildthread then computes `namepos = 1 + 20 + nframes*40 + ntbc*4` as a size_t. On LP64 that cannot wrap and `namepos > len` catches it, which is what I could test here. On a 32-bit size_t - wasm32, which section 17's own "An overflow that is unreachable on your machine is not unreachable" entry says this project ships - ntbc = 0x40000000 makes ntbc*4 wrap to 0, namepos passes, and ds_hook_finish (src/dsnap.c:1637-1643) reads ntbc to-be-closed slots with no bound of its own. I have no wasm32 or 32-bit harness in this environment, so I am not claiming it; it is the same shape as the bug section 17 already records one layer down.

Also seen and deliberately not reported as findings: the two SIGABRTs the re-stamped fuzz produced against the unmodified tree are ltests.h lock-accounting assertions (ASan on a non-ltests build is clean), so they are debug-instrumentation artefacts rather than memory errors; and the milestone record's test counts have drifted systematically (doc:2169 "66 checks" vs 92 actual, doc:1958 "82" vs 87, doc:2020 "112" vs 142, doc:2367 "430 mutants" vs 429) - a pattern across three milestones rather than a defect in the code under audit.

### audit `hostile-guest`

Worktree was on a base commit predating the messaging work; I reset it to claude/diluvium-messaging-supervision-ba6zku (55966a6) first. Baseline is green: dv_check 152/0, dsnap_check 142/0, dshim_check 87/0, dtask_check 25/0, dhash_check 252/0, dvs_check 92/0, mp_cursor_fuzz 10/0, `make sanitize_checks` reports "all contract tests clean under asan+ubsan".

I wrote three hostile-guest harnesses, all built with -fsanitize=address,undefined and ASAN_OPTIONS=detect_leaks=1, using test/dvs_check.c's host verbatim (create = no-op, drive = one dv_run/dv_resume):
  /home/user/diluvium/.claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-10/test/dvs_attack.c   (broad sweep, 60+ scenarios)
  /home/user/diluvium/.claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-10/test/dvs_attack2.c  (--stamp-write/--stamp-read for the two-process stamp demo)
  /home/user/diluvium/.claude/worktrees/wf_ddad3a8f-479-10/test/dvs_attack3.c  (--budget, --budgeted-child, --deep N)
Build line: gcc -fsanitize=address,undefined -O1 -g -DLUA_USE_LINUX -Wl,-E -ldl -DMAKE_LIB -I.data -o dist/dvs_attackN test/dvs_attackN.c .data/dvs.c .data/onelua.c -lm

CORRECTLY REFUSED, with no crash, hang, leak or sanitizer report anywhere:
- op wrong type: integer, table, boolean, a 40-character string, absent -> "no op in the request" or "denied <op>"; nothing created.
- the message not a map at all: a bare string, an integer, an array, nil -> "no op in the request".
- code wrong type: integer, table, absent -> "no code in the spawn request". code of 100 KB -> "the request is too large" (the 8 KiB peek bound at dvs.c:1059), message dropped, nothing built. code just under 8 KiB -> spawns normally.
- caps: a non-empty map, {1,2,3}, {true}, a 15-deep nesting, 40 entries, a 200-character name, a plain string, a number -> all "malformed caps". caps = {} -> correctly read as the empty set (the empty-map-on-the-wire case). A 95-character name -> accepted and attenuation-checked. A name with an embedded NUL grants only the part before the NUL.
- attenuation, from a parent holding {"lifecycle","queue:work/*"}: "queue:*", "queue:", "*", "**", "q", "queue:workX" and "" were each denied by name; only "queue:work/*" and "lifecycle" were granted. I could not construct a widening grant through `implies`.
- a guest without `lifecycle`: its system/lifecycle is declared and written and never drained; nothing created.
- kill: id 0, own id, its parent, its sibling, 9999, -1, '1', {}, absent -> denied with a reason (see finding 2 for the ids that were not refused).
- hibernate: parent, sibling, 9999, 0 -> denied "not a descendant"/"no such instance" (see finding 3).
- 20,000 query / kill / spawn requests pushed in one step: no hang, no crash. Spawns throttle to the rate per step with one `throttled` event per step and the burst left in the guest's own queue, arriving in order over later steps; the table bound answers "the instance table is full".
- hibernating itself twice: hibernates, wakes on a push, re-hibernates with the pushed message intact inside the new snapshot.
- spawn code that will not compile, that errors immediately, that raises a table -> `faulted` with the message/traceback, nothing left behind. The child-fault path (the use-after-free fixed in 55966a6) is clean under ASan.
- 16 hand-built malformed msgpack lifecycle messages injected through dvs_push (0xc1; map32 claiming 0xffffffff pairs; str32 claiming 4 GB as key and as value; the two-array32 32-bit skip-overflow shape; caps as array32 claiming 4 GB; str8 claiming 255 with one byte present; uint64 id with the top bit set; NaN and 1e30 ids; a negative instruction budget; a nested map as the op; duplicate keys; ext 0x02 as the whole message; a truncated final pair; the empty message) -> every one refused by name.
- the wake buffer bound: 16 accepted, the rest DVS_LIMIT.

NOT REACHED / GAPS. I did not attack dendpoint.c, dqueue.c or dsnap.c directly except where the swarm drives them, so endpoint forgery and snapshot validation rest on the existing dv_check/snap_fuzz signal rather than on anything I ran. I did not test a multi-threaded host, so 11.4's threading contract is unexamined. The kill_subtree stack overflow (finding 8) I could only reproduce with a reduced stack limit; at the default 8 MiB the depth needed is order 10^5 live instances and I could not build that many. One incidental test-quality note that is not a product finding: test/dvs_check.c's guest programs pass `{cap = N}` to queue.declare, but the option is spelled `capacity` (dqueue.c:36), so every one of those queues silently gets the default capacity of 64 -- including the wake-buffer test whose output reads "(16 accepted, 48 refused of 64)".
